# Changelog

All notable changes to Mbumah Hardware POS are documented in this file (Keep a Changelog format; versions follow package.json).

## [2.294.0] The gates learn their window

The denied-gates report breathes over time — a 24h / 7d / 30d lens on the Most denied gates card, windowed through the route and voiced by the same pure lib. And the privilege-abuse engine counts aloud: from the third denial in the sliding window the 403 body carries the pure `lockoutWarning()` line plus the honest `attemptsRemaining`, so the warning lands before the 15-minute lock, not after.

- `deniedGateReport(total, gates, limit?, windowLabel = '24h')` — additive window label, every v2.293.0 voice pin green byte-for-byte.
- `/api/security/dashboard?range=24h|7d|30d` — whitelisted lens; both the resource groupBy and the window total read one `deniedGatesSince`; the response names its `window`.
- `lockoutWarning()` + `LOCKOUT_WARN_AT` in the pure permissions.ts home; `noteDeniedAndMaybeLock` returns `{ locked, attemptsRemaining, warning }`; all five 403 call sites thread the warning in.

## [2.293.0] The dashboard speaks the feed's language

The security dashboard joins the one-vocabulary law the bell's Security tab found in v2.292.0: same words, same tone, same home. And the denial ledger is now read back — a Most denied gates card shows which gates said no most.

### Added
- **The gate-heat report** (`src/lib/security-feed.ts` + `src/app/api/security/dashboard/route.ts`): the PERMISSION_DENIED rows grouped by the recorded resource (the gate's own address, written by `recordPermissionDenied`), the top gates surfaced on a new dashboard card with amber bars and per-gate counts. `deniedGateReport` — a pure lib function — voices the aggregate: top-5 by count (ties by path for determinism), the `namedDenials`/`unnamedDenials` arithmetic that always adds up to the 24h total, and a subtitle line that names the unnamed gap instead of silently dropping it. The honest-empty face: "No denials in the last 24h — every gate said yes."

### Changed
- **The one vocabulary completes** (`src/app/(shell)/tabs/security-tab.tsx`): the dashboard tab carried its OWN copy of `EVENT_TYPE_LABELS` (12 entries) and `SEVERITY_BG` (5 faces) — the same drift-waiting-to-happen the feed had before v2.292.0. The lib's map grows to the dashboard's twelve (RATE_LIMITED, CSRF_FAILED, INVALID_INPUT, SUSPICIOUS_ACTIVITY, SESSION_HIJACK_ATTEMPT join); the tab's local copies are dead; both surfaces import one home. The chart-fill palettes stay local — they are the v2.204.0 contrast-tuned recharts surface, not row faces.
- **The tone law learns CRITICAL** (`securityRowTone`): severity CRITICAL joins ERROR in the red family — the wire's loudest severity can never land on a quiet slate row. The dashboard's recent-critical rows now wear the same `SECURITY_TONE_CLASSES` verdict the feed's Security pane speaks: one function, two surfaces.
- **The severity badge faces** (`SECURITY_SEVERITY_BADGE_CLASSES`): the outline-badge triad lifted from the tab into the lib's closed five (CRITICAL/ERROR/WARN/INFO/DEBUG), contrast-safe -500-on-tint tokens per the v2.148.0 law.

## [2.292.0] Every denial lands on the record

A live-found security-observability bug, closed at the source: the role gates that refuse most denied requests never wrote the durable record — the security dashboard and the feed's Security tab could not see them. Now every denial lands on the record, and the security row's color is data.

### Fixed
- **The invisible denial** (`src/lib/auth.ts`): `requireAuth`'s ROLE gate wrote ONLY an ops systemLog — the durable SecurityEvent never landed. Reproduced live on :3200: a CASHIER session 403'd against `/api/security/dashboard`, `/api/users` and `/api/hr/employees` (five denials) and the SecurityEvent table stayed at count 0. `withSessionAuth` and `requireStoreAccess` carried the same gap; `requireRole()` — the in-handler helper — wrote NOTHING AT ALL. THE FIX: all three wrapper role-gates route through `recordPermissionDenied` (SecurityEvent + ops log + hash-chained audit row, swallow-all-errors — a denial must never 500 the route), filing `PERMISSION_DENIED` with permission `roles:<allowed>` so the feed's Security tab and the dashboard both see it.

### Added
- **The recorded twin** (`requireRoleRecorded`): the async form of `requireRole` that RECORDS the denial before refusing — same shape, one extra argument (the request, for the path/ip the record needs). The four in-handler call sites migrated: the leaves decision-capable gate (APPROVE/REJECT) and all three org-console diagnostics gates. The sync helper survives for non-recording callers, its doc now names the gap.
- **The security feed's one vocabulary** (`src/lib/security-feed.ts`, NEW): the abuse type set, the shop-floor labels, and the row-tone law move OUT of notification-center into ONE lib — the same one-vocabulary move the notification lens made in v2.290.0. The component imports, never copies.

### Styled
- **The tone law**: a security row's color is DATA. Severity leads (ERROR = red, WARN = amber — the row's own wire truth); when severity is absent the TYPE answers (the lockout family — ACCOUNT_LOCKED, BRUTE_FORCE — is the red family; every other abuse type amber); unknown stays quiet slate, never guessed into alarm. The one-red-for-everything face is gone.
- **The row speaks its record**: a blocked event wears its own quiet "Blocked" outline badge; the role and permission the recorder writes render as chips (permission in mono); the shield icon takes the row's tone; every mark stays inside the contrast-safe token set.

### Verified
- The tone table on the REAL `securityRowTone` (severity leads, lockout family by type, unknown quiet, every abuse type resolves, labels complete, faces closed-set) + the byte wiring (the recorder calls in all three gates, the death of the log-only blocks, the recorded twin's four call sites, the lib one-vocabulary, the row contract's severity/blocked). Older leaves pin re-anchored (same law, recorded bytes). Full gates green; live E2E: CASHIER denials now LAND in the ledger and the Security tab renders them with real content on both shells.

## [2.291.0] The HR shell boots its session; the badge opens the unread list

Two faces, one thread: the session the HR shell was silently missing, and the shortcut the badge was silently promising. The Security tab now appears on the HR workspace for the roles that own it, and the unread badge itself opens the feed straight onto the unread list.

### Fixed
- **The HR hydration gap** (`src/components/layout/hr-auth-boot.tsx`, NEW): nothing on the `/hr` route hydrated the auth store — the POS shell hydrates in `(shell)/page.tsx`, branch workspaces through the gateway, the HR shell through NOTHING. A direct load of `/hr` left `useAuthStore.user = null`, so the notification center's Security tab (role-gated SUPER_ADMIN/STORE_OWNER/BRANCH_MANAGER, v2.12.7) never rendered there — even though the session's own localStorage held the role. The fix is one line of wiring: the HR layout mounts the shared `useWorkspaceAuthBoot` (the same idempotent boot the root page runs — auth from localStorage, cookie adoption → `/api/auth/me`, app-store rehydrate), so every HR desk inherits a real session and the bell's store scope lands with it. No copy of the boot sequence exists.

### Added
- **The badge door** (`src/components/layout/notification-badge-button.tsx`, NEW): the unread badge is a BUTTON of its own. Clicking the bell still opens the feed on All (the general door); clicking the badge deep-links the feed ONTO the Unread lens — the question the pixel asks ("what are those 3?") is the question the click answers. One component, three mounts (expanded rail, collapsed rail, HR bell). A button may not nest in a button: the badge is a SIBLING in a relative wrapper, exactly where the old span sat.
- **The opener's lens law** (`src/lib/notification-filter.ts`): `openerLensFilter(open, wasOpen, initialFilter)` — every open lands on the lens its opener asked for, applied on the false→true transition ONLY; while the sheet is open the effect is a spectator and a lens the user pressed is never clobbered. Pure, so the transition table is runtime-pinned by value; the render sites wire it. The bell never inherits the badge's ask, the badge never inherits the bell's.

### Styled
- **The pill invites the hand**: the badge button carries the keyboard-first focus-visible ring (the v2.288 law), a `title` tooltip with the singular/plural law ("Open the 1 unread notification" / "…3 notifications"), and a gentle hover scale — the bounce-in still announces arrivals; the v2.148.0 contrast tokens (white on red-600, slate-950 on amber-500) ride inside the one shared component instead of three copies.
- **Honest names**: the bell keeps "Notifications (N unread)"; the badge says "N unread — open the unread list" — two stops, two destinations, no ambiguity for a screen reader.

### Verified
- The transition table on the REAL `openerLensFilter` (badge lands on unread, bell on All, spectator while open, close is quiet, every lens rides an ask). The byte net pins the boot island (shared boot, renders null, smuggles nothing), the badge contract, all three call sites, and the death of the inline span badges. Older suites re-anchored to the new shape without weakening a law (v2.148.0's contrast pins now sit at the source). Full gates green; live E2E on :3200.

## [2.290.0] The feed learns its unread lens

The badge's unanswered question: the bell's badge counts UNREAD rows, but the feed's filter row could only narrow by severity (All / Critical / Warnings / Info / Security / Email failed) — finding the 11 unread among 13 meant scanning by eye. This round the filter row gains the Unread lens, the badge's own chip, and every chip learns to speak its pressed state.

### Added
- **The unread lens** (`src/lib/notification-filter.ts`): the filter union and the pure predicate `applyNotificationFilter` — the feed's one filter home. 'unread' answers from the read ledger the caller hands in; with no ledger the lens returns the rows unchanged rather than inventing a read state. Severity, Security (v2.12.7) and Email-failed (v2.57.0) laws ride the same predicate for both the active and the dismissed list.
- **The Unread chip** (`src/components/notification-center.tsx`): the SECOND chip, right after All — the question "what are those N unread?" outranks every severity cut. Its count is the SAME number the badge speaks, so the chip and the bell can never disagree.
- **The live shed**: clicking a row marks it read; the read ledger updates, the memo recomputes, and the read row leaves the unread list in the same tick. The last read swaps the list for the feed's own positive moment: "No unread notifications / You're all caught up!". One implementation mounts on BOTH shells (POS sidebar + HR header bell).

### Styled
- **The chip speaks its state**: every filter chip is now an `aria-pressed` toggle (the codebase-wide lens convention), with the keyboard-first focus-visible ring (the v2.288 law) — keyboard is a first-class hand on the filter row too.
- **The dot's language**: the Unread chip wears the row dot's own mark — a quiet primary dot (never pulsing; the row dot already pulses and a chip must not shout), swapping to the chip's foreground when active so contrast holds.

### Verified
- The lens truth table on the REAL function (unread subsets, empty/full ledgers, the no-ledger refusal, 'all' order-preservation, severity/security/email-failed arms, both lists). Live E2E: the Unread chip sheds rows live, the caught-up moment lands, chip/badge counts agree, console clean.

## [2.289.0] The bell learns its key

The keyboard's quiet gap: the shortcut map has carried search (⌘K), the sidebar (⌘B) and the F-tab row since the redesign — but the notification feed, the surface the morning digests land in, had NO shortcut on any shell. (The "Notifications alt+T" hint in the accessibility tree is Radix's toast-viewport focus hotkey — it focuses visible toasts; it never opens the feed.) This round alt+N rings the bell on BOTH shells, and the help dialog finally names the row.

### Added
- **The bell's key** (`src/hooks/use-notification-hotkey.ts`): `useNotificationHotkey` + the pure predicate `isNotificationHotkey` — alt over the PHYSICAL N (`e.code === 'KeyN'`), meta/ctrl excluded (⌘/ctrl+N is the browser's new-window chord — never fight the browser), shift allowed (some intl layouts need it to reach the N). The match law lives on `e.code`, not `e.key`: option+N on macOS produces 'ñ'/'Dead' in the character — a character match would silently die on the most keyboard-rich platform.
- **One key, two openers, never a copy**: the POS sidebar (`app-sidebar.tsx`) wires the hook to its bell state; the HR header bell (`hr-shell-bell.tsx`, v2.288.0's island) wires the SAME hook to its own sheet — the feed opens with the keyboard from the shop floor and the workspace alike. Each opener is `useCallback`-stable, so the document listener never churns.
- **The help row** (`src/components/keyboard-shortcuts-help.tsx`): `Alt+N — Open notifications` (bell icon) joins the map between the F-rows and Esc — the feed was the one surface the shortcut map never named.

### Styled
- The help dialog's kbd-row anatomy is untouched (icon · description · bordered kbd chip); the new row inherits the hover pill and the muted-voice treatment so the map reads as one list, not a patchwork.

### Verified
- The predicate's truth table (alt+N rings; meta/ctrl refused; bare N and alt+M silent; the mac 'ñ' trap documented by pin). Live E2E: alt+N opens the sheet on /hr and on the POS dashboard, Escape closes, ctrl+alt+N and ⌘+alt+N open NOTHING (the browser keeps its chords), the help dialog carries 11 rows including Alt+N, console clean.

## [2.288.0] The HR shell hears the bell

The reach fix. A worker parked on `/hr/*` never saw the notification center — the bell lived in the POS shell's sidebar and top-bar, and the HR workspace header carried only ten links. The digest beacons (v2.286/287) paper over the gap, but the feed itself stayed a walk away. This round the HR header carries ONE bell that opens the SAME notification center sheet — same feed, same filters, same deep-link walks, same storeId — and the ten links finally speak their place: the desk you are on is visible at a glance.

### Added
- **The HR-shell bell** (`src/components/layout/hr-shell-bell.tsx`): a bell button in the HR workspace header mounting the real `NotificationCenter` (mark all read, severity filters, dismissed drawer, security tab, retry delivery, deep-link walks). The storeId is the app store's own; the badge rides the badge hook the sidebar already uses — same `['notification-count', storeId]` cache key, zero extra network. The feed query is `enabled: open`, so the mount costs nothing until the bell is rung. Deep links written into bell rows resolve through the app router, identically from inside the workspace they announce.
- **The active nav** (`src/components/layout/hr-shell-nav.tsx`): the ten links lift into a client island so the CURRENT desk wears the accent pill (`aria-current="page"`, `bg-accent font-semibold`). The active law is a pure predicate — exact match for the workspace root (`/hr`), exact-or-descendant for every desk, segment-boundary aware (`/hr/employeex` is never `/hr/employees`), empty hrefs refuse to guess. Query strings never participate: `?status=` is a lens on the page, not a different page.

### Styled
- **The badge speaks the sidebar's law** (v2.148.0): critical unread = white on red-600 (4.8:1), everything else = slate-950 on amber-500 (8.6:1); the bounce-in replays only on an INCREASE via the same prev-count ref pattern; the cap reads 99+; the accessible name carries the unread count (`aria-label` + `aria-haspopup="dialog"`).
- **The hairline divider** between nav and bell (`bg-border`, hidden on phones, `aria-hidden`): the feed's door reads as furniture, not as an eleventh nav item.
- **Focus rings** on every nav link and the bell (`focus-visible:ring-2 focus-visible:ring-ring focus-visible:ring-offset-1`): keyboard is a first-class hand in a ten-link header.
- **The right cluster wraps as one unit** (`ml-auto flex min-w-0 flex-1 flex-wrap items-center justify-end`): the v2.147.0 law — no horizontal scroll at 390px — holds with the bell aboard; the layout stays a server component (it owns the metadata), the two islands are client children.

### Fixed
- The HR workspace's reach gap: digest beacons could point at a desk whose feed was unreachable from that desk's own shell. The bell closes the loop — the workspace a notification announces is now the workspace the bell lives in.

## [2.287.0] The bell lights its landing tab

The beacon family's newest member: the reports desk itself. A digest bell has always landed on a reports tab (headcount, cost, absence, turnover, time to hire, statutory, leave) — but from across the desk the landing was invisible until you happened to sit on the right tab. This round the desk wears its unread bells: the trigger of a tab an UNREAD digest bell walks to wears the pulsing amber dot while you are elsewhere. And the schedule vocabulary moves to one client-safe seam, so the desk reads the same map the builder writes — one vocabulary, never a copy.

### Added
- **The unread-bell beacons** (`src/app/(shell)/hr/reports/page.tsx`): every digest-carrying trigger (Headcount, Employment cost, Absence, Leave balances, Turnover, Time to hire, Statutory, Schedules) wears the amber beacon dot while an unread, undismissed digest bell walks to it and the visitor sits on another tab (`tab !== X && digestBeacons.has(X)`); the beacon never speaks on the tab you are already reading. The schedules trigger also lights while a `?schedule=` highlight waits on its face (the v2.27.0 deep link without its tab word is still a landing).
- **The client-safe seam** (`src/lib/hr/report-desk-links.ts`): `HR_SCHEDULE_REPORTS`, `HrScheduleReportKey`, `REPORT_KEY_TO_TAB`, `REPORT_KEY_TO_URL` move out of report-schedule-service into a PURE module both the server (builder) and the client (desk) can import — the service re-exports everything at the ONE seam, so every existing importer keeps its exact path and the compiler still forces every landing.
- **The walk parser** (`src/lib/hr/report-desk-links.ts`): `digestWalkTab(actionUrl)` — a walk lights a tab only when the pathname is exactly `/hr/reports` and a non-empty `?tab=` names it. Performance-pool bells walk `/hr/performance`: not this desk, never this desk's beacons. A bare `/hr/reports` names no tab (the desk's default face is the page's knowledge — the lib refuses to guess).
- **The beacon set** (`src/lib/hr/report-desk-links.ts`): `digestBeaconTabs(items, readIds, dismissedIds)` — unread and undismissed bells only (the shared external store the notification center writes, so a bell read in the feed goes dark on the desk in the same tick), duplicate bells on one tab collapse into one entry, a drifted tab word is carried verbatim for the page to intersect with its own known tabs.
- **The feed hand** (`src/hooks/use-notification-count.ts`): the badge hook now hands its active items to consumers (`activeItems`) — the desk reads the SAME query instance as the badge (same cache key, zero extra network).

### Styled
- **The pulse** (`src/app/(shell)/hr/reports/page.tsx`): a bell is waiting for a reader, so its dot PULSES (`animate-pulse`) — the performance desk's v2.286.0 filter beacons sit still, and the two meanings stay distinguishable at a glance. Same amber family (`bg-amber-500 dark:bg-amber-400`), same `aria-hidden` quiet: the bell feed and the badge remain the accessible surface for unreadness — the dot is desk-level wayfinding for the eye.

### Fixed
- The schedule vocabulary no longer lives behind a server-only import: a client desk can read the same map the builder writes without pulling the database into the browser bundle (the seam the beacons needed; the re-export keeps every existing import path byte-identical).

## [2.286.0] The word names its pool

The symmetric-face round: v2.285.0 taught the goals tab to answer `?status=`; this round the REVIEWS tab answers the same word. The two vocabularies are disjoint (OPEN/ACHIEVED/MISSED/CLOSED vs DRAFT/SUBMITTED/ACKNOWLEDGED/CALIBRATED), so the word itself names its pool — a bare `?status=submitted` routes to the reviews tab, a bare `?status=missed` still routes to the goals tab. And the pool digest's bell learns where the mass sits: the digest's OWN by-status table names the dominant status and the walk carries it.

### Added
- **The symmetric face** (`src/app/(shell)/hr/performance/page.tsx`): the `?status=` read now serves BOTH faces — an explicit `?tab=` pins the face (the word filters within it only when it belongs to that face's vocabulary; a human-typed mismatch like `?tab=reviews&status=OPEN` lands on the named face, never dragged elsewhere), and a bare word routes itself by vocabulary. Case-lenient (a human-typed link is still a link), vocabulary-strict (an unknown word falls back to the desk's own state).
- **The vocabulary map** (`src/lib/hr/report-schedule-service.ts`): `DEEP_LINK_STATUS_VOCABULARY` reads the lib's OWN words (`REVIEW_STATUSES` from performance-service, `GOAL_STATUSES` from goal-service — never a copy), and only the two performance pools speak a status vocabulary; the other seven bells walk their plain faces.
- **The bell lands where the mass sits** (`src/lib/hr/report-schedule-service.ts`): `digestDominantStatus(digest, vocabulary)` reads the digest's OWN by-status table — the same rows the reader just read — and the walk carries it (`/hr/performance?tab=reviews&status=<dominant>`). Ties break to the first row in table order (both report builders march their statuses in vocabulary order, so the break is deterministic). An empty pool, an all-zero pool, an unparseable or negative count, or a drifted table walks the plain face — the bell never guesses.
- **The cross-link owns its deep link** (`src/app/(shell)/hr/performance/page.tsx`): `setUrlParam` — the mirror of the drop. `viewCycleReviews` now speaks its own `?cycleId=` in the URL (a reload re-lands exactly what the walk landed; a stale param from an earlier link can never win) and retires any `?status=` the walk just reset.

### Styled
- **The liftable chip, on the reviews face** (`src/app/(shell)/hr/performance/page.tsx`): an active review-status filter — however it arrived — wears the amber deep-link family both faces speak (full dark-mode set, hover deepen); its × lifts BOTH the filter and the `?status=` param that asked for it.
- **The beacon** (`src/app/(shell)/hr/performance/page.tsx`): a filter that lives on an unvisited tab is never invisible — the Reviews/Goals triggers wear the amber dot while a status filter waits on that face (`aria-hidden`; the chip on the face does the talking).
- **The keyboard is a first-class hand** (both faces): the deep-link chips gain a visible `focus-visible` amber ring; the count lines ("Showing X of Y") are now polite live regions, so a screen reader hears the number move when a lens is pressed.

### Fixed
- **The last partial clear** (`src/app/(shell)/hr/performance/page.tsx`): the generic review clear retires BOTH deep-link params (`?cycleId=` and `?status=`), and a hand on a review status chip retires the param first — the link speaks once; the reader's own pick owns the URL. A state the URL forgot was a state a reload betrayed.

### Tests
- `src/tests/performance-deeplink-v286.test.ts` (35 pins, BOTH nets): the vocabulary map by VALUE (runtime pins — the lib's own arrays, disjointness asserted), the `digestDominantStatus` truth table (dominant, tie, number cells, all-zero, drifted word, unparseable, negative, no digest/table/rows, empty vocabulary), the builder's suffix + storeId hand, the desk's routing law (face pin, bare-word routing, incumbent preserved), the reviews amber chip, `setUrlParam` + the cross-link ownership, the double-drop generic clear, both beacons, both polite counts. `schedule-reviews-v283.test.ts` + `schedule-goals-v2282.test.ts` re-anchored to the import/override shapes (green).

## [2.285.0] The goals answer their link

The deep-link parity round: the goal-attainment digest has been scheduleable since v2.282.0, but its bell walked `/hr/reports?tab=goals` — and the reports desk has no `goals` tab, so the known-tab read fell through and every reader landed on headcount silently. A bell that walks nowhere is a lying door. This round the goal pool's bell walks where the pool actually lives, the goals tab learns its own deep link, and "one honest clear" becomes law across both desks.

### Added
- **The bell's honest landing** (`src/lib/hr/report-schedule-service.ts`): `REPORT_KEY_TO_URL` gains `'goal-attainment': '/hr/performance?tab=goals'` — the goal pool's interactive home is the performance desk's goals tab (the override is consulted before `REPORT_KEY_TO_TAB`, whose `goals` fallback remains as the belt). The review-cycles override stands untouched.
- **The goals tab's own deep link** (`src/app/(shell)/hr/performance/page.tsx`): `?status=` walks the GOALS tab into a pre-filtered state — lenient about case (`?status=missed` is a human-typed link and still a link), strict about vocabulary (only a KNOWN goal status lands; an unknown word falls back to the desk's own state). A valid status lands the reader ON the goals tab — an invisible filter is a lie. Same deferred, directive-free shape as the `?tab=` and `?cycleId=` reads (the CI lesson pre-applied).

### Styled
- **The liftable chip** (`src/app/(shell)/hr/performance/page.tsx`): an active goal-status filter — however it arrived — wears the amber deep-link family the cycle filter has spoken since v2.284 (full dark-mode set, hover deepen); its × lifts BOTH the filter and the `?status=` param that asked for it.
- **The honest answer** (both desks): a pressed house that empties the view names the word it was asked for — "No goal wears missed under the current filters." / "No review wears calibrated under the current filters." — the PO lens-empty law, now on the goals desk and the reviews desk alike.

### Fixed
- **One honest clear** (`src/app/(shell)/hr/performance/page.tsx`): the param-drop lives in ONE helper (`dropUrlParam`), and every hand that retires a deep-linked filter retires the param with it — the generic review clear (`?cycleId=`), the generic goal clear (`?status=`), a hand on the cycle select, a hand on a status chip. A clear that leaves its param behind was a partial clear: the next reload re-imposed a filter nobody sees.

### Tests
- `src/tests/performance-deeplink-v285.test.ts` (18 pins, BOTH nets): the override map by VALUE (runtime pins), the case-lenient/vocabulary-strict read, the goals-tab landing, the chip's amber anatomy and word-naming lift, the one-honest-clear law (helper + four retiral hands), the lens-empty law on both desks. `schedule-pool-scope-v284.test.ts` re-anchored to the helper shape (13 pins, green).

## [2.284.0] The pool speaks its true scope

The scope-honesty round: the review pool is organization-wide BY CONSTRUCTION (the cycle and review models carry no storeId), so a branch-scoped `review-cycles` schedule would compute org-wide counts while wearing a branch's name — a lying document. This round the narrowing is refused at the door, the digest's scope line is forced honest, the constraint becomes visible on the schedules desk, and the performance desk's deep links learn `?cycleId=`.

### Added
- **The create door** (`src/lib/hr/report-schedule-service.ts`): `validateScheduleInput` rejects `review-cycles` + a branch scope outright — `INVALID_SCOPE`, 422, "The review pool is organization-wide — schedule it at the organization, not a branch." The narrowing is refused, not silently corrected. Branch-scoped reports keep their doors.
- **The belt** (`src/lib/hr/report-schedule-service.ts`): the review-cycles digest case forces `scopeLabel: 'All branches (organization)'` — if a branch-scoped row ever exists anyway, the document STILL speaks its true scope. (The goal pool never had the hole: `employeeScopeWhere` narrows the goal aggregate honestly.)
- **The walk continues** (`src/app/(shell)/hr/performance/page.tsx`): `?cycleId=` pre-filters the reviews tab on landing (the same deferred, directive-free shape as the `?tab=` read — the v2.283.0 CI lesson pre-applied), and an active cycle filter wears a liftable amber chip whose × drops BOTH the filter and the `?cycleId=` param — the URL stays truthful; a reload after the lift must not re-impose a filter nobody sees.

### Styled
- **The dialog's locked scope** (`src/components/hr/report-schedules-tab.tsx`): picking an org-pool report locks the branch pick to "All branches (organization)" (disabled select, forced value, the hint swapped to "The review pool is organization-wide — every cycle and review, whatever branch employs them") — the 422 becomes un-hittable from the wire, and the constraint is visible BEFORE submit, not an error after.
- **The card's org-pool mark** (`src/components/hr/report-schedules-tab.tsx`): an org-pool schedule wears a quiet slate "Org pool" chip beside its frequency badge — the operator sees the digest speaks the organization before opening it. Slate is the quiet family (SKIPPED/CLOSED/DRAFT); no colour in use gains a second meaning.

### Tests
- `src/tests/schedule-pool-scope-v284.test.ts` (13 pins, BOTH nets): the scope rejection's truth table (INVALID_SCOPE + 422 for review-cycles + a store; accepted at the organization; branch reports keep their doors), the forced scope line, the dialog's lock bytes, the card's mark, the deferred `?cycleId=` read, the liftable chip's amber family and URL-truthful clear.

## [2.283.0] The cycles join the digest

The performance module's second parity face: v2.282.0 taught the goal pool to speak and the handover named the review pool as the next gap. This round the cycles/reviews aggregate joins the governed digest seam — scheduleable, previewable, emailable through the ONE seam every surface mirrors — and the digest preview learns to speak the pool vocabularies' own colours.

### Added
- **The review pool's aggregate** (`src/lib/hr/performance-service.ts`): `reviewCycleReport(session, now)` — counts ONLY (the spec's mask is stricter here than for goals: ratings never ride a list payload, a fortiori never an email; the aggregate speaks how many, never how good); the governed scope is `listCycles`' scope (every non-deleted cycle of the organization, the non-deleted reviews of those cycles) but NOT its `take: 60` — that is a console page size, not a scope, and the digest is the standing pool; rows march `REVIEW_STATUSES`' own order (DRAFT, SUBMITTED, ACKNOWLEDGED, CALIBRATED) each with its count and pool share; `reviewCyclePct(part, whole)` — the ONE share, exported for the runtime pins (null when the pool is empty or negative — a percentage over nothing is a guess); `unclosedWindows` — the clock's own count of ACTIVE cycles whose window has ended but which were never closed (the boundary is STRICT: a window that ends this instant is not yet over). NO FALLTHROUGH RENAMING: a status word the vocabulary does not know buckets AS ITSELF — tracked > the rows' sum is how a stray shows itself.
- **The digest case** (`src/lib/hr/report-schedule-service.ts`): `review-cycles` joins `HR_SCHEDULE_REPORTS` (label "Review cycles"); the case builds the headline (Tracked cycles / Active cycles / Tracked reviews / Awaiting acknowledgement / Calibrated / Unclosed windows) and the "Review pool by status" table, `periodLabel` "All cycles — the standing pool" (year-independent like the goal case); an empty pool digests as honest zeros with the share face's own `—`.
- **The deep link** (`src/lib/hr/report-schedule-service.ts` + `src/app/(shell)/hr/performance/page.tsx`): the review pool's interactive home is the PERFORMANCE desk, not the reports desk — `REPORT_KEY_TO_URL` overrides are consulted before `REPORT_KEY_TO_TAB`, and the desk now reads `?tab=` on mount (only a KNOWN tab id lands; an unknown word falls back to cycles). The digest's bell action walks the reader into the reviews tab.

### Styled
- **The preview's face** (`src/components/hr/report-schedules-tab.tsx`): the email-style strip wears the report's OWN glyph (the same face the schedule card shows — preview and card scan as one object; the glyph varies, the sky family never does); the digest table's label column speaks in the pool vocabularies' own colours — a KNOWN lifecycle word wears its chip (amber waiting, emerald positive, rose negative, slate quiet, sky in motion — five BORROWED families, no word wears two colours across the app), an unknown word stays plain text (the document exposes what it cannot name, never dresses it up); the numeric columns right-align under their own headers so digits of the same magnitude scan down a column instead of zig-zagging.
- **The schedule door** (`src/components/hr/report-schedules-tab.tsx`): the report select grows "Review cycles (all cycles)" and the review pool wears the reviewer's own clipboard glyph.

### Tests

## [2.282.0] The digest learns the goals

The performance module had no digest face: the scheduler covered seven HR reports and none of them goals. This round the goal pool's aggregate joins the governed digest seam — scheduleable, previewable, emailable, CSV-able through the ONE seam every surface mirrors — and every schedule card learns to speak its report's own glyph.

### Added
- **The goal pool's aggregate** (`src/lib/hr/goal-service.ts`): `goalAttainmentReport(session, now)` — counts and sums ONLY, the same governed scope `listGoals` obeys (`employeeScopeWhere`), the same Number()-per-row door the leave report opens; rows march the vocabulary's own order (OPEN, ACHIEVED, MISSED, CLOSED) each with its target sum, current sum and attainment percentage; `goalAttainmentPct(target, current)` — the ONE percentage, exported for the runtime pins (null when the target sum is 0 — a percentage over nothing is a guess; a NEGATIVE target is data corruption, not a denominator); `overdue` — the clock's own count of goals the ledger still holds OPEN past their due date (the pool saying "these look missed, and nobody has re-run me yet"; the boundary is STRICT). NO FALLTHROUGH RENAMING: a status word the vocabulary does not know buckets AS ITSELF — the document exposes what it cannot name, never renames it.
- **The digest case** (`src/lib/hr/report-schedule-service.ts`): `goal-attainment` joins `HR_SCHEDULE_REPORTS` (label "Goal attainment", CSV slug `goals`); the case builds the headline (Tracked goals / Attainment / Achieved / Missed / Overdue) and the "Goal pool by status" table, `periodLabel` "All cycles — the standing pool" (year-independent like the leave case); the honest `—` face where a percentage would be a guess; an empty pool digests as honest zeros.

The GLYPH varies, never the colour: every icon stays the schedule's own sky family; Mail stays the fallback and keeps the dialog's "Would email" row (which is about the email).

## [2.281.0] The card remembers its deliveries

The Schedules card spoke only its LAST send (`lastStatus` on the row), and the v2.25 delivery dots stayed lazy — hidden behind the history panel's fetch. An operator scanning the list could not see "3 of the last 5 sends failed" without opening every card. This round the card remembers its deliveries: the list view carries each schedule's delivery health (the last five recorded sends — counts, entries, ONE spoken line) derived server-side from the send ledger, and every card wears an always-on health strip.

### Added
- **The card's delivery memory** (`src/lib/hr/schedule-health-voice.ts`, import-free — the client-bundle law): `deriveSendOutcome(status, hasError)` — the ONE derivation (SENT + error = **partial**, the batch reached only some recipients; PENDING and any word the vocabulary grows later stays pending — an unresolved row is not a verdict); `scheduleIdFromMetadata` — the correlation's single parse; `aggregateScheduleHealth(logs, scheduleId, window)` — newest-first entries over the five-send window, counts, and the honest absence (no rows → null — the face never dresses an empty ledger as "0 delivered"); `scheduleHealthVoice` — the ONE line ("4 delivered · 1 failed across the last 5 sends", singular keeping its plain form "across the last send"). `HEALTH_WINDOW = 5`, `HEALTH_LOG_CAP = 250` (50 schedules × the 5-send window is the exact need).
- **The governed read** (`src/lib/hr/report-schedule-service.ts`): `listSchedulesView(organizationId)` — the list plus one bounded `NotificationLog` read whose OR-of-contains correlation is governed BY CONSTRUCTION (the schedule ids come from the actor's own list, so the query can never widen past the actor's scope; NotificationLog has no organizationId to join through, and no new table is invented for what the log already remembers). Metadata parsed once per row; the pure voice aggregates; health rides the view ONLY where sends exist. The catch-quiet law: a failing log read returns the bare list — the cards speak their lastStatus as before. `toScheduleView` stays pure of the log read (create/PATCH/fire flows unchanged).
- **The always-on strip** (`src/components/hr/report-schedules-tab.tsx`): beneath the last fire's faces — one dot per send, oldest-left (the voice's newest-first entries, reversed for reading order), each dot titled "outcome · date", and the voice as real text. `data-testid="schedule-health-{id}"` + `data-window`; per-dot `schedule-health-dot-{id}-{n}`. The v2.25 lazy dots row retires INTO this face (the history panel keeps its full per-row detail).

### Styled
- **The colour collision retires**: the v2.25 dot map gave PARTIAL and PENDING the same amber; partial keeps amber (`bg-amber-500`, the mixed-delivery state the chips already speak), pending wears quiet slate (`bg-slate-400`) — colour never means two things. Delivered emerald, failed rose — the chips' own families. The strip's hover title names the deeper surface ("open Delivery history for the full per-recipient detail").

### Tests
- `src/tests/schedule-health-v2281.test.ts` — 38 pins: the outcome truth table (the partial law, the benefit of the doubt), the single metadata parse's honest absences, the voice's grammar (order law, non-zero parts, singular form, the zero-window silence, the all-zero tail), the aggregation's ordering/grouping/window-cap/count laws and its voice agreement, the pure file's import-free law, the service's governed read (the OR-of-contains shape, the cap, the catch-quiet law, health attached ONLY in the list flow), the route riding the view, and the card's strip bytes (testids, the reversal, the four colour families, the v2.25 retirement).

## [2.280.0] The schedule keeps its appointment

The Schedules card has always spoken its LAST fire (the status chip and its date); the NEXT fire hid inside the preview dialog — an operator had to open a dialog to learn when the morning digest would land. This round the card keeps its appointment: the row view carries `nextRunAt`, derived server-side through the same pure law the nightly sweep and the preview already obey, and the card wears an appointment strip beneath the last fire's faces — the scheduled face, or the honest paused face when no fire is scheduled.

### Added
- **The row's own appointment** (`src/lib/hr/report-schedule-service.ts`): `ScheduleView` grows `nextRunAt: string | null` — `toScheduleView` derives it through `nextScheduleRunAt` (the ONE derivation; the card's "next fire" can never disagree with the nightly cron's own math) and keeps the honest absence for a paused schedule (`null` = no fire is scheduled until reactivation, never a guessed instant). The seam feeds every surface that renders a schedule row (the list GET, the create POST, the PATCH) — one derivation, every surface mirrors.
- **The appointment's pure voice** (`src/lib/hr/schedule-fire-voice.ts`): `relativeFireLabel(iso, now)` — "today" / "tomorrow" / "in N days" / "N days ago" by the UTC-midnight floor law (25 hours ahead is TOMORROW, never "in 2 days"; the filing voice's own calendar arithmetic), malformed instants keeping the honest absence (''); and `fireImminent(iso, now)` — the sky dot's gate, true when the fire lands today or tomorrow. Import-free (the client-bundle law).
- **The appointment strip** (`src/components/hr/report-schedules-tab.tsx`): the fire box grew a second row — an active schedule wears "Next fire · {date}" with the relative chip ("in 7 days"), a paused schedule wears "Paused — no fire while paused."; both faces carry `data-testid="schedule-next-fire-{id}"` and their `data-state`. The last fire's faces (status chip, error, re-send affordance, health dots) keep their bytes.

### Styled
- **The sky family, the schedule's own colour**: the imminent dot rides the card icon's and header gradient's family — held `bg-sky-500` when the fire lands today or tomorrow, quiet `bg-sky-300` otherwise; the relative chip wears the sky outline. Colour never means two things — emerald/rose/amber stay the delivery verdicts. The fire ledger mates seamlessly: the top box drops its bottom border and rounding only when the strip renders beneath it. The empty Schedules tab grew its own door — "Create the first schedule" sits where the reader lands, not only in the header.

### Tests
- `src/tests/schedule-fire-v2280.test.ts` — 26 pins: the relative voice's calendar law (today/tomorrow/yesterday/marches, the 25-hour-is-tomorrow edge, the midnight edges, the honest absence), the imminent gate's truth table, the ONE-derivation agreement (`toScheduleView(row).nextRunAt === nextScheduleRunAt(row).toISOString()` across WEEKLY/DAILY/MONTHLY, the never-fired row still speaking, the paused row's null), the seam's and card's byte pins, and the pure file's own laws (the floor lives once; import-free).

## [2.279.0] The email hears the clock

v2.278.0 put the receipt counts in the digest's table — but the email still opened on the money, and its faces were type-generic grey. This round the digest email opens on the CLOCK and wears the receipt's own family: the next deadline derived at the fire instant rides the headline first, and a payroll month whose composition is complete is marked emerald in the HTML by the same law that writes "4 of 4 filed" in the CSV.

### Added
- **The clock chip** (`src/lib/hr/compliance-calendar.ts`): `digestNextDeadlineChip(now)` — the NEXT deadline derived purely from `complianceCalendar` at the fire instant ("9 November 2026 · October 2026 payroll"), spread FIRST in the statutory headline so the morning email answers what's coming before what was owed. Words only — the chip borrows no urgency tones (the desk's card owns the window arc and the status colors). The chip rides every twin (HTML headline, CSV header block, plain-text lines) through the ONE seam. Computed once per fire, spread once.
- **The paired face** (`src/lib/hr/compliance-calendar.ts`): `digestFilingComplete(month, counts, now)` — the voice's boolean twin, through the SAME window gate and the SAME clamps; and `filingWindowOpenDay(month)` — the window's open day-key extracted so the gate lives ONCE (the voice's inline math retired; the `utcDayKey(now) < openDay` law byte kept). THE AGREEMENT LAW (pinned as a truth table): complete ⇔ the voice reads the complete composition ("4 of 4 filed" with its optional amendment tail) — the email's emerald and the CSV's words can never disagree.
- **The good cells** (`src/lib/email-templates.ts` + `src/lib/hr/report-schedule-service.ts`): `HrDigestTable.goodCells?: Array<[row, column]>` — COORDINATES only, emitted by the builder through `digestFilingComplete` (column 7, the Filed face). The HTML email renders marked cells in `BRAND.primaryDark` (emerald-600 — the receipt's family per color-never-means-two-things) at weight 600; the CSV and text twins never read goodCells (plain by law); a payload without goodCells renders byte-identical to the pre-v2.279 template (pinned).

### Styled
- **The email's good family**: the marked Filed cell wears the SAME emerald the shell's brand already speaks (`#059669`, emerald-600 — no new palette), exactly one colored cell per complete month; the clock chip renders as the headline's plain words; the low-stock badge precedent (conditional faces in email) extended by coordinates rather than string-sniffing — the mark travels as data, never as a guess.

### Tests
- `src/tests/digest-email-clock-v2279.test.ts` — 23 pins: the ONE gate (the voice rides `filingWindowOpenDay`, the inline math gone), the agreement truth table over the gate (nulls, partials, amendments, clamps, unopened windows, malformed months), the chip's honest date pairs (on-or-before the 9th reads this month's 9th over the previous payroll — the calendar's own window law; past it, the roll), the December roll, the always-derivable calendar, the builder's once-computed chip + coordinates + the v2.278 rows bytes untouched, the email's emerald-td bytes with the exactly-one-colored-cell law, the byte-identical no-goodCells render, and the plain-text twin's colorless law. `panel-portal-v2275.test.ts` evolved deliberately (the import grew the paired faces — named in the ancestor's own body).

## [2.278.0] The digest learns the receipt

v2.275.0 named the digest's statutory column "Due by", never "Filed", because the ledger did not exist; v2.276.0 built the ledger; the desk's Due cell learned to count receipts (v2.276.0/277.0) — but the digest, the surface the filing officer actually reads each morning, stayed silent. This round the mirror completes: the digest's statutory table gains the Filed column, COUNTS only, through the same seam that feeds the scheduled email, the ad-hoc CSV and the desk's own table CSV.

### Added
- **The digest's receipt voice** (`src/lib/hr/compliance-calendar.ts`): `digestFilingVoice(month, counts, now)` — the document's-answer grammar in order: counts null → '' (a ledger that cannot speak is never guessed); a window that has not opened (now before the 1st of the due month, the census's UTC-midnight floor law) → '' (the clock governs the first record — a "0 of 4" cried over an unopened window would be neglect where none is possible); otherwise "N of 4 filed", with " · K amended" when the slack law's corrections ride the receipts. Both counts clamp at the door (a corrupt payload cannot inflate a composition — the RUNTIME-pin law). The denominator is `STATUTORY_OBLIGATION_KINDS` — the CLOCK's own list, exported from the calendar (the ledger's STATUTORY_FILING_KINDS speaks what the ledger accepts; the two agree today, each answering its own concern, and the pure calendar may never import the ledger's Prisma graph). `filingWindowOpensForPeriod(month)` derives the open day from `statutoryDueForPeriod`'s own due date — the ONE derivation, never a second 9th-minus-8 math.
- **The composition rides the report** (`src/lib/hr/reports.ts`): `statutoryLiabilityReport` grows `filings` — per payroll month, how many of the four obligations hold a receipt and how many of those were genuinely amended (`filingWasAmended`'s slack law decides, not the eye). The read is the ledger's OWN read (`fetchStatutoryFilings` — one home, never a second roll call), scoped to the month range the monthly rows already span; a receipt whose payroll month has no row here is another report's story; a ledger read that cannot speak (pre-migration database, any failure) leaves the composition null and the money still tells the truth (the load-erasure law). COUNTS only — names, references and notes never ride a report.
- **The seam clothes every surface** (`src/lib/hr/report-schedule-service.ts`): the statutory case's columns grow 'Filed' beside 'Due by' and each row's last cell is `digestFilingVoice` at the builder's own `now` (the scheduled fire's instant). The same case feeds the scheduled email, the ad-hoc CSV and the desk's table CSV — one seam, every surface mirrors. The v2.275 comment tells the new truth where the law changed.

### Styled
- **The receipt rail on the desk's Due cell** (`src/app/(shell)/hr/reports/page.tsx`): the single emerald check dot (the all-four gate `monthFullyFiled` pinned since v2.276.0) grew into the four-dot RECEIPT RAIL — one dot per obligation in the calendar's own order (PAYE, NSSF, SHIF, housing levy), emerald-500 when the ledger holds that receipt, quiet slate-300 when it does not, riding `monthInCoverage` so a cell keeps quiet outside the read window (the law the rail inherits, never retires). `role="img"` aria-label speaks the ONE grammar the hover title already speaks ("filed: N of 4 obligations · K receipt(s) amended") — the dots carry the face, the aria carries the composition, and no new palette is invented. `monthFullyFiled` retired (the rail IS the composition); the per-dot testids (`hr-due-filed-dot-paye` …) join the rail's own (`hr-due-filed-rail`).

### Tests
- `src/tests/digest-filings-v2278.test.ts` — 28 pins: the RUNTIME laws (the window-open derivation against `statutoryDueForPeriod`'s own bytes, the December→January and January rolls, the open-day edge, the four grammars with amendment tails, the clamps at the door, the honest absences — null counts, unopened windows, malformed months), the read's composition (the ledger's own read, the slack law, the cross-month continue, the catch-quiet, the no-rows skip), the seam's eight columns and the fire-instant voice, the aggregate law at the seam, the desk rail's bytes, and the two ancestors' named evolutions. `panel-portal-v2275.test.ts` and `filing-ledger-v2276.test.ts` evolved deliberately (the seam learned the receipt; the dot grew the rail — each named in the ancestor's own body).

## [2.277.0] The chip takes its correction

v2.276.0's route supported amendment (the compound unique makes a re-record an AMEND, and the audit vocabulary already knew `STATUTORY_FILING_AMENDED`), but the card had no door for it — a filed receipt was a dead end on the desk. This round the chip itself becomes the amendment door: the clock keeps governing the FIRST record (a window that has not opened has nothing to file — the law holds), and the LEDGER governs corrections — a receipt that exists can be amended whenever the filer needs, and the correction starts from the truth it corrects.

### Added
- **The chip is the amendment door** (the Statutory tab's filing calendar): a filed receipt chip is now a button (`aria-haspopup="dialog"`) whose click opens the receipt dialog in AMEND mode — every field prefilled from the ledger's own row (filed-on through the ONE field math, reference, note), so a correction never starts from a blank. The record door ("Mark filed", window-gated) rides unchanged beside it.
- **The dialog's two modes**: record (the window's door — datetime defaults to now, submit reads "Record filing" with the stamp) and amend (the chip's door — the dashed emerald strip speaks the original recording, who and when, the submit reads "Update filing" with the pen). The route's `{filing, amended}` answer lands as the card's quiet amended note — a NEW receipt announces itself (the chip appears); an AMENDED one changes nothing the eye can see, so the note speaks it.
- **The receipt's own history rides the row** (`src/lib/hr/statutory-filing.ts`): `StatutoryFilingRow` gains `createdAt`/`updatedAt` (ISO on the wire; the schema already carried the stamps — no migration this round); the read and the write both select and map them. The chip's title says "amended <date>" when the row was genuinely amended, and the monthly figures' Due titles count corrections the way they count receipts ("filed: 4 of 4 obligations · 1 receipt amended").
- **The pure helpers on the pure home** (`src/lib/hr/compliance-calendar.ts`): `toDatetimeLocalValue` — the datetime-local value's ONE math (local-offset, minute precision; the record mode's now-default and the amend mode's prefill can never disagree about what an instant looks like in a field) — and `filingWasAmended` — the slack law (same-write timestamps within one second are the receipt's BIRTH, never a correction; a row that cannot speak its history is not claimed as corrected). Both RUNTIME-pinned; both live on the import-free calendar lib (the client-bundle law).

### Styled
- **The door's affordance, quiet and named**: the chip keeps the emerald "the ledger holds the receipt" family (a color never means two things) while gaining the door's manners — cursor-pointer, hover deepens the face (`emerald-100`/`emerald-800`, 4.5:1 held), focus-visible rides the emerald ring; the amend strip is the dashed emerald face that says "this receipt was recorded …" so a correction always knows what it corrects; the amended note is the small check line that never shouts; the pen (amend) and the stamp (record) never share a submit.

### Tests
- `src/tests/filing-ledger-v2277.test.ts` — 21 pins: the RUNTIME laws (toDatetimeLocalValue's minute-precision round-trip and honest empty; filingWasAmended's slack, birth-not-correction, and honest absence), the row's history through both selects, the chip's door bytes (button, haspopup, prefilled open), the dialog's two modes (titles, strip, submits), the amended note riding the response's own flag, the Due cell's amendment count, the window law unchanged, and the ancestor's four named evolutions. The treadmill walked 316 ancestor pins across 71 files (run once; the pair map verified by git diff — a doubled execution re-eats the pair through the head map, the DOUBLE-RUN LAW's scar re-earned and recovered by checkout + single re-walk).

## [2.276.0] The ledger takes the receipt

v2.274.0's card footer said "whether a window was filed is the ledger's business" — deferring to a ledger that did not exist. This round the law completes: the filing ledger exists. A receipt is the filer's assertion — what was filed, when it actually went in, an optional reference — recorded from the desk where the deadline is already watched, audited at the door, and spoken back by the chip and the monthly figures' dot.

### Added
- **The filing ledger** (`prisma/schema.prisma` + `prisma/migrations/20261030070000_add_statutory_filings`): `StatutoryFiling` — one row per (organization, obligation kind, payroll month), the compound unique making a re-record an AMEND rather than a second truth. The filer's FK rides `ON DELETE SET NULL` (a deleted account never strands a receipt, the stocktake precedent); the tenant FK cascades. The committed migration is postgres-dialect (the house style); the vercel-build's `sync-db-schema.mjs` applies it on deploy, and the route fail-closes to `FILING_LEDGER_UNAVAILABLE` on a pre-migration database — the precise code, never a bare 500.
- **The ledger's one home** (`src/lib/hr/statutory-filing.ts`): the pure gate `validateFilingInput` (kind ∈ the calendar's four, month ∈ "YYYY-MM", filedAt never in the future beyond one minute of clock skew, the reference and note capped at source, trims at the door); the org-scoped read whose select refuses the filer's id and carries the filer's NAME (this desk already speaks names at roster distance); the upsert write that says whether it recorded or amended. `periodMonthForDueDate` derives the payroll month from the calendar's ISO due date — the ONE join key the card's chips, the monthly figures' dots, and the dialog's submit all speak, so a chip and a dot can never disagree about which month a receipt covers.
- **The audited door** (`src/app/api/hr/reports/compliance-filing/route.ts` + `HrEntityType.STATUTORY_FILING`): GET and POST both ride the calendar's drill-down guard; the write's audit action distinguishes `STATUTORY_FILING_RECORDED` from `STATUTORY_FILING_AMENDED`, and the metadata carries field names and month keys only — the voucher number never rides the audit trail.
- **The card wears the receipt** (the Statutory tab's filing calendar): a filed obligation wears the emerald receipt chip (`filed <date>`, the filer and reference in the title) beside its status chip; an unfiled, open window wears the dashed "Mark filed" door (a window that has not opened has nothing to file); the dialog takes the filer's assertion (filed-on datetime defaulting to now, optional reference, optional note) and the ledger answers before the chip appears. The footer now speaks both halves: "The clock speaks deadlines; the ledger speaks receipts."
- **The monthly figures learn the composition** (v2.275's Due column): within the read window the cell's title counts the receipts ("filed: 2 of 4 obligations" / "no filing recorded"); outside it the cell keeps quiet — no coverage, no claim. A month with ALL FOUR receipts wears the emerald check dot.

### Styled
- **A color never means two things — the receipt's face named**: on the filing calendar card, emerald means "the ledger holds the receipt" (the good family — the census's emerald lives on the Portal tab, a different desk); the clock's rose/amber/sky stay the clock's own. The Mark filed door is the dashed-border quiet affordance that greens on hover; the dialog's fields carry the desk's h-8 rhythm and the error speaks the route's precise code; the Due cell's dot is the small inline check that never shouts. 4.5:1 contrast holds on every new face.

### Tests
- `src/tests/filing-ledger-v2276.test.ts` — 25 pins: the RUNTIME gates (the kind/month/future/caps laws with the skew edge), the join key's backward reading and the January→December roll, the migration's compound unique + SET NULL laws, the schema's back-relations, the route's twin guard + audited actions + values-never-in-the-trail, the card's chip/button/dialog bytes, the loader fold (no new set-state-in-effect family member), and the Due cell's coverage-quiet law. The v2.274 footer pin evolved deliberately (the law completed, the evolution named in the ancestor's own body); the treadmill walked 316 ancestor pins across 71 files (run once, verified by git diff).

## [2.275.0] The expert hears the census

The census had two of its three doors: the reports desk speaks it (v2.271.0), the org console wears it (v2.273.0) — but the shared People & Payroll panel, the one component BOTH the Expert Console and the Finance tab render, still stayed silent, one release after the parity gap became visible. This round the panel hears the census. And the statutory desk's money table learns what the calendar taught it: every payroll month's row now names the deadline the statute sets.

### Added
- **The panel wears the census column** (`src/components/finance/people-payroll-by-branch.tsx`): the shared "People & payroll by branch" table gains the Portal column — `linked/total` with the rate in the census's own emerald and the roster bell riding the cell, one click opening the adoption census narrowed to that branch. The cell IS the door on BOTH homes at once (the Expert Console's collapsed panel and the Finance tab's open one) — one implementation, two mounts, exactly the law the panel was written under. The mobile card mounts the SAME cell (one vocabulary, never two), the honest dash renders for a branch with no employees in scope, and the rate comes from the pure `portalAdoptionPct` (the RUNTIME-pin law: a corrupt payload cannot inflate a rate — linked clamps into the scope; the rate of nothing is null, never a guess).
- **The ledger learns the clock** (`src/lib/hr/compliance-calendar.ts` + the Statutory tab + the digest): `statutoryDueForPeriod(month)` derives any payroll month's deadline — the 9th of the following month, the same Date.UTC construction `complianceCalendar` uses, so a card and a row can never disagree — from the month key the monthly rows already carry (no wire change). The reports desk's monthly statutory figures gain the resizable "Due" column (weekend 9ths speak the settle-before words and wear the amber); the digest's statutory table gains "Due by" (ISO, so the CSV never argues about locale) through the SAME `buildDigestPayload` case that feeds the scheduled email and the ad-hoc table CSV — one seam, every surface mirrors. The column is named "Due", never "Filed": whether a past window was actually filed is the ledger's business, and no surface claims a receipt.
- **The href has ONE writer** (`src/lib/workspace/links.ts`): `buildPortalReportHref(storeId)` joins the links lib — the org console's drill-down cell (v2.273.0) and the shared panel's PortalCell now speak the SAME builder, so the two doors can never disagree about where the census lives. The composed bytes are identical to the inline template the ancestor pinned (the evolution is named in the ancestor's own body).

### Styled
- **A color never means two things**: the portal cell wears the census's emerald (`text-emerald-700 dark:text-emerald-400` + the emerald focus ring) — amber stays the expiry trail's, orange stays the panel's hover, sky stays the calendar's open window. The panel's header enumeration now says what the table shows ("headcount, cost, absence, latest run, portal adoption, expiry alerts per branch"), the Due cell keeps the desk's tabular numerals with the weekend amber carried from the calendar, and the 4.5:1 contrast laws hold on every new face.

### Tests
- `src/tests/panel-portal-v2275.test.ts` — 23 pins: the RUNTIME arithmetic (the rate's clamps and honest nulls; the 9th-of-the-following-month derivation, the December→January roll, the weekend law cross-checked against the engine's own weekday computation, the malformed month key's honest null), the ONE-writer builder (composed bytes, URL-encoding, the org console's retirement of the inline template), the panel's byte law (the door testid, the emerald face, the two-mounts-one-cell rule, the enumeration), and the desk+digest mirror (the Due column's resize spread, "Due" never "Filed", the digest's ISO seam).

## [2.274.0] The calendar keeps the clock

The statutory desk has always spoken the money — the liability totals and the monthly figures say what was charged — but it never said WHEN: Kenya's monthly payroll obligations fall due on the 9th of the following month, and that clock lived in nobody's head but the filing officer's. This round the desk keeps the clock: a filing calendar derived from the server's own time, one row per obligation, the window state up front and the weekend warning when a 9th lands on a Saturday or a Sunday.

### Added
- **The pure calendar** (`src/lib/hr/compliance-calendar.ts`): `complianceCalendar(now)` derives the four monthly obligations (PAYE, NSSF, SHIF, housing levy — the 9th-of-the-following-month family) as a pure function of a point in time. The census's UTC-midnight floor law computes the day differences (a 25-hour gap is 1 day, never 2); the window law reads on-or-before-the-9th as the previous payroll month's open window and after-the-9th as NOT_YET_OPEN with the clock rolled to the next deadline; the weekend law cites the weekday when a 9th lands on a Saturday or Sunday — the statute does not wait. The calendar cites its instruments and never claims a receipt: whether a window was actually filed is the ledger's business, and the payload's spoken words cannot say otherwise (pinned).
- **The read route** (`src/app/api/hr/reports/compliance-calendar/route.ts`): the same `hrGuard('hr.report.branch')` drill-down roles, no period params (the clock is not period-scoped), no storeId (the statute does not vary by branch), no audit (reads ride the guard alone) — the payload is one `asOf` instant, the point-in-time law the census reports by.
- **The filing calendar card** (`src/app/(shell)/hr/reports/page.tsx`, Statutory tab): the clock renders ABOVE the money — a calendar-page tile per obligation (day over month), the period and due date spoken plain, a window arc showing how much of the 1st–9th window has burned, and the status chip carrying the page's own family: rose acts now (due today / due tomorrow), amber warns (2–4 days), sky informs (open), and the unopened window stays muted — an unopened window is not an emergency and never borrows one's colors. The card owns an INDEPENDENT load (`loadCalendar`, the loadErasure pattern): a period-report failure darkens the money, never the clock.

### Tests
- `src/tests/compliance-calendar-v2274.test.ts` — 13 pins: the RUNTIME window law (mid-window, the 1st edge, DUE_TODAY at the 9th, the clock roll past it, the December→January year roll, the census floor law at 23:59), the four obligations sharing the statute's day while keeping their own names and instruments, the weekend law (derived Saturday/Sunday months from a fixed anchor, with the Monday-9th silence pinned), and the surface laws (a guard-riding read with no audit, the independent load, the family tones).

## [2.273.0] The console hears the census

v2.271.0 rolled the roster's quiet bell up to a census, and v2.272.0 gave the census its own audited copy — but the census still lived one navigation away from the desk where adoption is actually decided. The organization console's People & payroll strip answered headcount, cost, absence, payroll and expiry — everything except the adoption story the leave and payslip emails assume. This round the console hears the census: the numbers ride the org wire, the drill-down wears the column, and the CSV files the fifth section.

### Added
- **The census on the org wire** (`src/lib/hr/reports.ts`): `hrSummaryForOrgConsole` fetches three counts in the census's OWN where-shape (deletedAt null + scopeWhere — the tile can never disagree with the report it opens) and derives the five census numbers through the pure `portalAdoptionCounts` (the RUNTIME-pin law: the vitest pin caught a clamp against the RAW linked that let an active login exceed the clamped linked — fixed at source with ladder-order clamping, the invariant holds by construction). `hrPerStoreForOrgConsole` groups the census denominators per store (linked = holds a user row; in scope = non-deleted — never the ACTIVE-only headcount, a different denominator would lie). Three COUNT queries and two groupBys — never a second roll call; the tile needs the numbers, not the names.
- **The console's adoption tile** (`src/components/workspace/org-console.tsx`): a sixth "Portal adoption" StatCard in the People & payroll strip — the rate as the value, "N of M linked · K not linked" as the footer, one click to `/hr/reports?tab=portal` (report distance). A scope with no employees keeps the plain muted face.
- **The drill-down's Portal column**: "People & payroll by branch" gains a sortable Portal column whose cell IS the door — `linked/total · rate%` opening `/hr/reports?tab=portal&storeId=…` (the drill-down law every cell keeps), the honest dash for a branch with no employees in scope. The comparator (`src/lib/org/hr-drilldown-sort.ts`) sorts the RATE the cell wears: loudest-first fresh click, directional linked-count tiebreak, and the payroll absence rule carried over — the rate of nothing is no comparison, so the dash sorts LAST in both directions. The column rides `defaultDirFor`'s own rule (the pinned plain-field family keeps its exact members).
- **The CSV's fifth section** (`src/lib/org/branches-csv.ts`): `buildBranchesCsv` grows an optional `portal` section — the SAME sorted rows the table speaks (the file-mirrors-screen rule), the adoption % computed BY THE LIB from each row's own linked/total (round2 of ×100, the reviewer-share precedent), the honest empty rate cell for a scope of nothing, and a self-explanatory Note line. No portal rows → byte-silence: the file ends exactly where v2.177.0 ended. The export note counts the section it actually wrote.

### Styled
- **The tile wears the census's own emerald**: StatCard's tone vocabulary grows `'good'` — `text-emerald-700 dark:text-emerald-400`, the SAME pair the report tab's chips and meters speak (a color never means two things; warn stays amber). The adoption tile's small print keeps the 4.5:1 contrast law the amber label earned in v2.149.0. The drill-down header's enumeration now says what the table shows: "headcount, cost, absence, latest run, expiry alerts, portal adoption per branch".

## [2.272.0] The census carries its copy

v2.271.0 gave the reports desk the adoption census — but a census you cannot file is a census you cannot take to the meeting. The digest export route refuses the roll call ON PURPOSE (a digest is aggregate-only by construction; the roll call is name-bearing), so a name-bearing file gets its OWN door: same guard, same scope law, its own audit action.

### Added
- **The census's audited CSV door** (`src/lib/hr/portal-adoption-csv.ts` + `src/app/api/hr/reports/portal-adoption/export/route.ts` + `src/app/(shell)/hr/reports/page.tsx`): a "Roll call CSV" button on the roll call card downloads the governed file — the house format (header block, Summary of the census numbers, the roll call table), built by a PURE builder that shapes only what the ALREADY-governed census DTO carries (the OWASP formula-injection guard is `csvCell` itself; RFC-4180 CRLF; an honest point-in-time filename). The route rides the same `hr.report.branch` guard + roles, the same `validateReportStore` drill-down, refuses an empty census with the honest 404 `NO_REPORT_DATA`, and writes its own audit action `PORTAL_ADOPTION_EXPORTED` → DataExport with COUNT-ONLY metadata (rows, scope, byteSize, channel) — the roll call's names never ride the audit trail, and the file has no email column because the census never fetched one. An empty census refuses to file; the digest export route stays aggregate-only, untouched.

### Styled
- **The door wears the census's own emerald** (`border-emerald-200 … focus-visible:ring-emerald-400`): the linked-login color, never the digest's violet — a color never means two things. The button spins (`Loader2`) with honest "Building…" words while the file builds, its `role="status"` note speaks the size and the audit law when it lands (or the failure in the shared red), and the whole door is `print:hidden` like every export control.

## [2.271.0] The report hears the bell

The roster answers WHICH hires hold portal access (v2.268.0's quiet bell) and the lens can ASK for them (v2.270.0) — but the reports desk had no census. This round the bell is rolled up to report distance: a point-in-time adoption roll call over the governed scope, on its own tab of /hr/reports.

The service's select REFUSES the email (role + active state only — the shaper cannot leak what the query never fetched); the rows carry exactly the shaped bits the roster wire carries. The census reloads when the branch chip narrows the surface (the storeId law every report table keeps), speaks its failures with the shared retry strip, and has no digest CSV by design (a census has no period to schedule).

The by-branch meter is a thin emerald gradient bar with an aria-label speaking the rate aloud.

## [2.267.0] The portal's keystone

Linking a login to an employee — the keystone the whole self-service phase stands on — got its honest faces. The QA walk found the gap at the wire: the portal-link POST/DELETE route admits SUPER_ADMIN, STORE_OWNER and HR_MANAGER, but the payroll tab (visible to all of MGMT_ROLES) fetched its picker from GET /api/users, a route that only ever answers the first two tiers. A BRANCH_MANAGER or ACCOUNTANT opening the link dialog got a 403 they never saw — the list rendered empty and the empty list lied ("No active users found for this store").

### Added
- **The candidates wire** — `GET /api/employees/[id]/portal-link/candidates`: the picker's own governed read, gated by the link route's own trio, scoped to the employee's store, ACTIVE accounts only, ordered by name. Every login already bound to another employee is annotated with where it is bound (`linkedToEmployeeName`), so the picker disables the row with its reason instead of silently hiding it. Reads live in the employee service (spec rule 15); the route is a thin honest voice.
- **The detail wire grows `portalUser`** (additive, optional): `getEmployeeDetail` includes the linked account through the shaped `HrPortalUserDto` — the raw relation key is destructured out of the spread, only the DTO leaves the server.
- **The Portal account card** on the HR employee detail page: the binding (name, mono email, role chip in the shared palette, active state) with its Unlink pen for the trio, the honest amber absence with a candidates picker + Link pen, the audit footnote, and the service's own messages verbatim (role=status / role=alert).

### Fixed
- **The silent empty list** (payroll tab): the dialog's picker now reads the candidates wire; roles without the trio get the honest denial panel ("Your role can view this link but not change it — only store owners, HR managers and super admins manage portal account links") instead of "No active users found". The Unlink button renders only for the trio — a read-only role no longer gets a button whose only outcome is a 403 toast.
- **One palette, one gate, one shaper**: `portalRoleTone`, `canManagePortalLinks`/`PORTAL_LINK_ROLE_TRIO` and `buildPortalCandidates` live in `src/lib/hr/portal-account.ts` (client-safe) and both surfaces share them; the client-side `linkedUserIds` mirror retired with the old fetch.

The count is the CALENDAR gap between the day the supplier named and the day the order was raised: twenty-five hours past the named midnight is one day after, never two; a raise later in the named day itself speaks its own words.

No second listener — the detail's Escape is Radix's own, the topmost layer answers first, and the ladder's silence law holds by the platform's own stack.

## [2.257.0] The escape ladder

The drills could be opened by keyboard and read by keyboard, but they could only be closed by mouse — the ear's guard (v2.256.0) correctly hands Escape to "the dialog", and the drill WAS the dialog, so a press on an open drill did nothing. This round the one home grows the drills' own rung: Escape closes the drill you are reading, the ladder stacks cleanly, and the law is spoken where the hand can find it.

### Added
- **The drills' rung** (`src/lib/use-lens-escape.ts`, the one home): `useDrillEscape(onClose)` — Escape closes the drill when it is the tree's ONLY dialog; when another dialog rides above it, the drill stays silent and the topmost dialog's own law speaks first. The guard reads the live tree at the moment of the keypress (the ear's law mirrored, never a copy), and the listener's lifetime IS the drill's mount (unmount disarms).
- **Both drills wear the rung** (`po-drill.tsx` + `sheet-drill.tsx`): one import, one call, one home — the PO drill and the sheet drill close on Escape exactly as their overlay click and close button already speak.
- **The ladder stacks**: a drill over a pressed lens gives Escape → the drill closes (the drill's rung), Escape again → the lens stands down (the ear's rung, whose guard bytes are unchanged). One press pops the topmost layer, always.
- **The esc hint** (both drill headers): a quiet `esc` kbd chip beside the close button (`aria-hidden` — the close button's own label carries the law), and the close buttons' titles grew the words "Escape closes this drill".

### Styled
- The close buttons' rings joined the focus-visible law: violet for the PO drill (its door's family), amber for the sheet (its icon's family) — keyboard focus is visible, mouse clicks leave no ring.
- A type-only import cleanup landed across seven files (`consistent-type-imports`, the lint audit's auto-fixable tail, bundled per the v2.256.0 handover): inline `type` modifiers on type-only imports and one forbidden `import()` annotation restructured in the payroll lifecycle test — no behavior moved.

### Tests

- **The birth's door** (`po-drill.tsx` + `po-ledger.tsx`): the birth strip's "open the sheet" button hands the parent the SheetDrill's own target shape — `{id, storeName, status}` read from data the wire already carries (the sheet rides the birth, the store name rides the PO's own store) — and the ledger opens the sheet drill as the PO drill closes (ONE drill at a time; the reading moves, nothing fetches twice). A dialog's Escape belongs to the dialog: the guard reads the live tree (`[role="dialog"]`) at the moment of the keypress, never a cached answer. The ear sleeps while the lens is down.

## [2.251.0] The walk's when

The birth strip learned the aisle's name at v2.248.0 and the org drill learned its cadence chip at v2.250.0 — but the branch strip still answered only WHERE the walk order pointed, never WHEN that aisle was last heard, and the ledger's file spoke the aisle's name without its voice. The band is not a cost: it rides for every viewer.
- **The branch birth strip's chip** (`purchase-orders-tab.tsx`): the violet strip wears the cadence chip — `bandHouse`'s byte-identical classes, `daysVoice` on the title, `chipLabel`'s word — `data-testid="po-detail-aisle-band"` (the org drill's `po-drill-aisle-band` sibling). A band the read could not answer stays absent — never a guessed house.
- **The org list wire's row bands** (`GET /api/org/purchase-orders`): the roster's sheet-born rows answer when their walk aisle was last heard — ONE stored-law read, then ONE band read per DISTINCT aisle id in the window (the dedupe keeps the cost at the page's aisle spread, not the row count; each read is the census's own three bounded queries). Rows' `walkAisle` grows `band` + `daysSince`.
- **The ledger CSV's aisle_band column** (`rosterCsv`): the file grows `,aisle_band` beside `,aisle` — the walk's WHEN, the four houses the wire already computed (zero new fetch). A row whose read failed or whose birth had no aisle exports empty (a file has no titles — the word speaks alone; the days stay the screen's voice).

## [2.250.0] The branch's gate

The cost gate comes down to the branch: the org wires have spoken the shillings' law since v2.227.0 and the products wire since v2.12.2, but the branch purchase-order wires never grew the gate — every viewer with store access read the roster's totals, the document's financial block and every line's cost. - **The branch detail wire's gate** (`GET /api/purchase-orders/[id]`): the document answers the same truth — the header's three money fields, the lines' `unitCost`/`totalCost`, the product's book cost — `null` past the gate, `Number()` past it.
- **The aisle's band on the birth strip** (`GET /api/org/purchase-orders/[id]` + `po-drill.tsx`): the walk order's own cadence chip rides the violet strip — `readCategoryBand` (the census's single-aisle read, the stored law, one home) answers fresh/stale/beyond/never, and the chip wears `bandHouse`'s byte-identical classes. A read that fails is silence — never a guessed house.

### Fixed

The pen now starts EMPTY (the user's own typed truth) and the stock line speaks units alone.

## [2.249.0] The birth's ledger

The walk's answer went up to the org face: a restock born at a count sheet has worn the violet birth on the branch roster since v2.248.0, but the org's procurement ledger — the face that watches every branch's pipe — read every row the same. This round the ledger learns the birth, the drill names the sheet, and the door prices the hole.

### Added
- **The org wire's birth** (`GET /api/org/purchase-orders`): the rows grow `originStockTakeId` + `originStockTake` (additive + optional — the SAME shape the branch wire speaks: id, status, closedAt, the aisle's name). A hand-raised PO answers null and renders the silent absence. The wire grows the BIRTH RUNGS: `births: { sheet, hand }` beside the status ladder — two indexed counts over the rung-LESS narrowing (status + branch), so pressing one rung keeps its sibling's real number (a rung that zeroed its sibling would trap the reader).
