The `origin` param narrows the rows: `sheet` / `hand`; anything else is a REFUSED filter (the same 400 law as status — an unknown rung never silently means "all").
- **The org detail wire's birth** (`GET /api/org/purchase-orders/[id]`): `originStockTake` rides the read (additive + optional, the same shape) — the drill names the sheet.
- **The ledger's birth rungs** (`po-ledger.tsx`): a second rung row — "born at sheets N" (the violet family, the birth chip's own house) and "hand-raised M" (the slate family) — `aria-pressed`, the toggle law the status rungs speak, the query key carries the rung (a rung change IS a refetch).
- **The ledger's birth chips**: each sheet-born row wears the violet chip beside the PO number ("born at a sheet", the aisle's name on the title); a hand-raised PO renders the silent absence — the type check, never an assumption.
- **The org drill's birth strip** (`po-drill.tsx`): a sheet-born document opens wearing the violet strip — the sheet's id, its aisle when it wore one, the honest status voice — and, past the cost gate, the restock's own shillings (the summary tile's gated total, spoken where the birth is spoken). A hand-raised PO renders the silent absence.
- **The restock door's shillings** (`sheet-drill.tsx`): past the cost gate AND only when EVERY shrinkage line carries its own `varianceCost`, the door names the hole's book-cost price (`KES 2,250.00 at book cost` on the pickaxe's sheet). A partial sum would understate and lie — an incomplete set keeps the units-only voice.
- **The roster's birth column** (`rosterCsv`): the file grows `,birth` — every row speaks `sheet` or `hand`. A FILE has no chips, so the word speaks for every row; a file's silence would be an absence, and a hand-raised PO is a known state, not a missing one.

### Styled
- The violet family grows its org faces: the rung's pressed half (`bg-violet-600` / dark `bg-violet-300` on `violet-950`), its hover half, the chip's 3:1-on-both-halves rings, the strip's border families — `tabular-nums` on every figure, focus rings in the rung's own house (violet for the sheet rung, sky for the slate rung), dark halves from birth.

## [2.248.1] The restock's honest shillings

The E2E birth caught its own bug: the create dialog's pre-pick read `l.product?.costPrice` — a field the sheet detail wire has NEVER carried (the wire maps each line's gated cost to `bookCost`, and no nested product rides the lines). Every pre-picked restock line landed at unitCost 0.

### Fixed
- **The pre-pick's cost** (`purchase-orders-tab.tsx`): the pre-pick reads the line's own `bookCost` — the wire's gated cost voice. A viewer holding `inventory.view.cost` gets the real shillings pre-filled; a viewer without it gets the units pre-picked and the cost left for the pen (the units are the truth, the shillings follow the gate — never a guessed zero).

## [2.248.0] The door's answer

Two doors, one law: a door must land where it points, and a finding must be answerable. This round fixes the first with the workspace's tab landing (a real bug: every branch-workspace door anchored to whatever tab localStorage had persisted), and builds the second — the restock's birth, the walk lifecycle's missing closure: the shrinkage a walk found can now be ANSWERED by a purchase order born wearing the sheet's name.

### Added
- **The restock's birth (schema)**: `PurchaseOrder.originStockTakeId` (nullable, indexed, `SetNull` on the sheet's deletion) + the `StockTake.restockOrders` read-side relation. A deleted sheet nulls the column — the PO never speaks ghosts. A hand-raised PO carries no birth at all.
- **The restock's wire** (`POST /api/purchase-orders`): the create wire grows the BIRTH ALLOWLIST — `originStockTakeId` is stored ONLY when the sheet exists, belongs to the SAME store, and is CLOSED; a bad address is a 400 REJECTION, never a silent null (an origin is explicit intent, not inference). The list + detail wires grow `originStockTake` (additive + optional: id, status, closedAt, the aisle's name).
- **The restock door** (`sheet-drill.tsx`): a CLOSED sheet that found shrinkage (lines whose count came in BELOW the snapshot) wears a violet anchor beside the recount door — "raise the restock for the N shrinkage lines" — into `/stores/{slug}/purchase-orders?restockFrom={sheetId}`. Violet = the door's family (the birth chip's own house). Absent when the sheet found no shrinkage, is not CLOSED, or has no slug — an absent control never lies.
- **The restock's landing + pre-pick** (`purchase-orders-tab.tsx`): the tab reads `?restockFrom=` ONE time at mount (the count sheet door's own one-time law), auto-opens the create dialog, and pre-picks exactly the shrinkage lines (qty = the units the shelf lost, cost = the product's own cost price riding the sheet wire). The violet BIRTH STRIP speaks while building; a refusing sheet speaks its refusal — the birth stays off the record.
- **The birth's faces**: the PO roster wears the violet birth chip beside the number, the PO's own record wears the birth strip (sheet id, aisle, honest status voice) — for life.
- **The doors' landing (bug fix)**: the workspace page forwards its catch-all segments; the gateway maps the first through `tabFromWorkspacePath` (src/lib/workspace/links.ts) and sets the tab once at ready. `/stores/{slug}/inventory` OPENS inventory — the walk doors, the recount door and the restock door are deterministic for every browser, not just the ones whose persisted tab happened to agree. An unknown or absent segment leaves the persisted tab standing (the old behavior, byte for byte).
- **The laws named (ride-along)**: the census row's title says its verdict sums every closed sheet born here this window; the walk picker's findings title says the LATEST walk's own truth — the two faces can no longer be mistaken for each other.

## [2.247.0] The walk's mirror


An older door-born sheet could wear its aisle's latest verdict without saying so — the v2.246.0 line spoke the AISLE's truth under THIS sheet's header, and a reader could mistake it for the sheet's own finding. This round the sheet's own walk speaks first: when this paper's walk found something DIFFERENT from what the aisle's latest window walk found, both truths ride the drill and the branch face. When they agree, the single honest line stays — a duplicate would say nothing new.

### Added
- **The mirror's read** (`GET /api/stock-takes/[id]` + `GET /api/org/stock-takes/[id]`): both detail wires grow `walkAisleSheetUnits` (additive + optional) — the sheet's OWN spoken units in its aisle, the lib's own testament law mirrored back at the sheet itself: ONE bounded aggregate, indexed by the sheet's id, the same product-category join + variance-not-null shape `readCategoryBand` speaks. THE UNITS LAW: units are units (the cost gate is NOT involved); a perfect count speaks 0 — the shelf held this walk; nothing spoke (no counted lines on the aisle) → null, the honest absence, never a hidden zero.
- **The mirror's faces** (`sheet-drill.tsx` + `stock-take-panel.tsx`): when both truths are present AND differ, the faces speak this walk's own verdict FIRST (`sheet-drill-origin-aisle-self` / `stocktake-sheet-origin-aisle-self`, the lib's `findingsVoice` — the same three families and colours as the census — `tabular-nums`, the paper's own truth on the title), with the aisle's latest beneath it. SIBLING LINES, NEVER REWRITES: the v2.246.0 verdict line stays byte-identical. Absent when either truth is absent, or when the two agree — the silence is the honest state.

## [2.246.0] The aisle's testament

The sheet's aisle has said WHEN it was last heard since v2.244.0 — this round it says what its latest window walk FOUND there: the aisle's testament rides the detail wires, and the drill and branch faces wear the verdict line. The walk lifecycle's faces now all speak the same truth: the pick strip says it before (v2.245.0), the census says it org-side (v2.243.0), and the sheet itself carries it after. And the verdict's words and colours move into their one home — the anti-duplication law applied to the findings voice itself.

### Added
- **The testament's read** (`src/lib/cadence-band.ts`): `readCategoryBand` grows `lastWalkUnits` — the categories wire's own law mirrored for one aisle. The latest SPEAKING line in the window (a line with variance non-null; a line the floor never counted stays silent), then that sheet's spoken units in THIS category (one aggregate, the same product-category join — line-level attribution, the shelf's own truth). THE UNITS LAW: units are units (the shillings gate is NOT involved). A perfect count speaks 0 — the shelf held. Null when no window walk spoke (beyond / never / a silent window) — the honest absence, never a hidden zero. THE READ CLASS: the band's one line read stays first; the testament adds two bounded reads, in the window only. THE FALLBACK LAW per read: a band that fails is no band (null, as before); a testament that fails under a living band is silence (units null, the band keeps its truth).
- **The testament's wire** (`GET /api/stock-takes/[id]` + `GET /api/org/stock-takes/[id]`): both detail wires grow `walkAisleUnits` (additive + optional), read only for an aisle-born sheet. The band + days lines stay byte-identical (the v2.244.0 pins keep speaking).
- **The testament's faces** (`sheet-drill.tsx` + `stock-take-panel.tsx`): a verdict line under the sheet's when-line on both faces — `sheet-drill-origin-aisle-verdict` / `stocktake-sheet-origin-aisle-verdict`, the census's own three families via the lib, `tabular-nums` on the figure, the shelf's own truth on the title. SIBLING LINES, NEVER REWRITES: the chip row stays byte-identical. Absent when the wire carried no units — the silence is the honest state.

### Changed
- **The findings voice's one home** (`findingsVoice` in `src/lib/cadence-band.ts`): the census's three families (exact = muted slate "the count held — 0 units", surplus = emerald "+N units found", shrinkage = red "−N units found") had lived as two copies — the map's inline verdict and the pick strip's local voice. One home now: the map's verdict rows, the pick strip's verdict line and the detail faces' testament lines all speak through the lib. The v2.243/v2.245 pins rode the move (the documented hand-pass — their TRUTH moved, their intent preserved).

### Styled
- The verdict lines ride the census's exact families and voices — one law, four faces (the map's aisle rows, the pick strip, now the drill and the branch face), `tabular-nums` on every figure, dark halves from birth, no new paint. The honest absences hold: a beyond/never aisle wears band but no verdict; an aisle-less birth wears neither.

## [2.245.0] The walk's findings

The pick face has said WHEN a shelf was last heard since v2.236.0 — this round it says what the last walk FOUND there: the units the latest window walk's lines in that category landed, in the census's own three families. The decision moment gets the full truth: blind spot or fresh, and what the last count landed. Plus one real bug fix: the picker's beyond voice had kept a hardcoded "30 days" — a constant the v2.241.0 law retired.

### Added
- **The findings wire** (`GET /api/categories?includeCadence=true`): the cadence map grows `lastWalkUnits` per category — the LATEST window walk that actually SPOKE for the shelf (a line with variance non-null; a line the floor never counted stays silent), and the units its lines in THIS category landed (line-level attribution — the shelf's own truth, not the whole sheet's). THE ZERO-NEW-QUERIES LAW: the attribution rides the pairs the cadence already reads — the `variance` column added to the existing `windowLines` read, nothing else. THE UNITS LAW: units are units (the shillings gate is NOT involved). A perfect count speaks 0 — the shelf held. Null when no window walk spoke (beyond / never / a silent latest) — the honest absence, never a hidden zero.
- **The findings face** (`stock-take-panel.tsx`, the pick strip): a verdict line under the band chip + days voice — `stocktake-pick-cadence-verdict`, the drill's own three families (exact = muted slate "the count held — 0 units", surplus = emerald "+N units found", shrinkage = red "−N units found"), `tabular-nums`, the shelf's own truth on the title. The block's aria carries the findings. Absent when the wire carried none — the silence is the honest state.

### Fixed
- **The beyond voice reads the stored law** (`pickDaysVoice`): "not heard in 30 days" hardcoded a window the v2.241.0 law made governable — the line now says `not heard in {windowDays} days` from the wire's own number (the demo law speaks 5/45, so the face now says 45). The teeth-v2236 pin rode the truth change (the documented hand-pass).

### Styled
- The verdict line reuses the census's exact voice strings and families — one law, three faces (the map's aisle rows, the drill's line chips, now the pick strip), tabular-nums throughout, dark halves from birth, no new paint.

## [2.244.0] The aisle's echo

The aisle's row on the map has testified since v2.243.0 — this round the sheet itself carries the echo home: open a door-born count sheet (org drill or branch face) and its origin-aisle line learns the aisle's own cadence band. WHERE the door opened (v2.242.0) now says WHEN its aisle was last heard. And the four band houses move into their one home — the anti-duplication law applied to the band itself.

### Added
- **The band's one home** (`src/lib/cadence-band.ts`): the four houses (fresh emerald / stale amber / beyond orange / never slate), the days voice, and the chip label move from the variance map's local closures to a shared lib — byte-identical classes, the map's call sites unchanged. The lib also grows `readCategoryBand`: ONE aisle's band, read the map's way (the latest CLOSED line touching a product currently on the aisle; no line → never; in-window → fresh/stale by THE LAW'S OWN boundary; older → beyond). THE FALLBACK LAW: a failed read returns null — never a thrown request, never a guessed house.
- **The echo wire** (`GET /api/stock-takes/[id]` + `GET /api/org/stock-takes/[id]`): both detail wires grow `walkAisleBand` + `walkAisleDaysSince` (additive + optional), read only for an aisle-born sheet (two bounded reads: the stored law + the ONE latest CLOSED line). The `walkAisle` name line stays byte-identical (the v2.242.0 pins keep speaking).
- **The echo's face** (`sheet-drill.tsx` + `stock-take-panel.tsx`): a sibling band chip after the name chip on both faces — `sheet-drill-origin-aisle-band` / `stocktake-sheet-origin-aisle-band`, the house's classes, the days voice on the title, `tabular-nums` on the figure. SIBLING SPANS, NEVER REWRITES: the name chips and the birth chip stay byte-identical. Absent when the wire carried no band — the silence is the honest state.

### Styled
- The band chips wear the exact houses the cadence rail has spoken since v2.235.0 (8px, uppercase, ring-1, dark halves from birth) — one home now, so the drill, the branch face and the map can never drift apart. The name chip keeps the violet door family untouched; the band chip is the aisle's own truth, not the door's paint.

## [2.243.0] The aisle's verdict

The census named the aisles the doors opened onto (v2.242.0) — this round the aisle's row speaks the walk's own VERDICT: the variance units the window's closed door-born sheets found, and the aisle's current cadence band (did the walk move the needle?). The arc completes its sentence: the door points, the floor answers, the aisle is heard — and now the aisle testifies.

### Added
- **The aisle's verdict** (`GET /api/org/kpi-summary`): the `doorBornByAisle` rows grow three additive fields — `varianceUnits` (the closed door-born sheets' variance UNITS, attributed to the aisle the DOOR named via the sheet's own `walkCategoryId` — no product join, no guess; two bounded reads: the closed door-born sheets, then their spoken lines), `band` + `daysSince` (the aisle's own cadence band, joined from the cadence the same wire already computes — two Maps, zero new queries). THE UNITS LAW: units are units (every org role hears them — the shillings gate is NOT involved). A counting sheet has not spoken; a sheet closed with a perfect count speaks 0 — the shelf held. Optional per field (an older cached answer renders the birth-only row).
- **The verdict's face** (`variance-map.tsx`): each aisle row speaks its verdict under the share bar — `cadence-aisle-verdict-{categoryId}`, right-aligned, `tabular-nums`, in the drill's own three families (exact = muted slate "the count held — 0 units", surplus = emerald "+N units found", shrinkage = red "−N units found"); the aisle's band rides the name line — `cadence-aisle-band-{categoryId}`, the walk order's own four houses (fresh emerald / stale amber / beyond orange / never slate), the days voice on the chip's title; the row's aria-label carries the whole testimony (births, band voice, verdict).

### Styled
- The verdict line and the band chip ride the map's existing families, never new paints: the band chips are the `bandHouse` classes the cadence rail already speaks (8px, uppercase, ring-1, dark halves from birth), the verdict line mirrors the drill's `varianceChip` three-family law (slate/emerald/red, dark halves), and every figure stays `tabular-nums`. The honest absences hold: a walk still out (counting, not closed) wears band but no verdict line; an aisle the cadence never knew wears no chip.

## [2.242.0] The aisle's answer

The census has counted HOW MANY sheets were born at a walk door since v2.238.0, and per branch since v2.239.0 — this round it learns WHERE: the walk order pointed at an aisle, and now the census names the aisles the doors actually opened onto. The door arc's final altitude: the point, the floor, the yard, and now the aisle — one violet family, five altitudes.

### Added
- **The aisle's answer** (`POST /api/stock-takes` + `prisma/schema.prisma`): a door-born sheet remembers the aisle its walk order named. `StockTake.walkCategoryId` (nullable, additive; migration `20261028230000_add_stock_take_walk_category`) rides ONLY when the sheet was born at a walk door AND the birth named one category — THE AISLE LAW: a hand-raised sheet carries no aisle even when it names a category (the census counts doors that named aisles, never shelves), and a door-born sheet whose floor flipped to the full sheet speaks no aisle (the birth sent none). THE GHOST LAW: the id is resolved server-side against THIS store — a dead or cross-store category reads as no aisle — and a deleted aisle nulls its sheets' column (optional relation, SetNull): the census never speaks ghosts.
- **The census per aisle** (`GET /api/org/kpi-summary`): one grouped read over the SAME window births the headline already speaks (aisle-named only), names via ONE catalog read (ids bounded by the group), and the face speaks the TOP 3 — the census is a headline, not a ledger. `doorBornByAisle` rides the payload additively (an older cached answer renders the silent absence).
- **The aisle's echo** (`GET /api/stock-takes/[id]` + `GET /api/org/stock-takes/[id]`): the detail wires grow the door's aisle name (`walkAisle` — the aisle's own name, additive; null when the birth named no aisle), and both faces speak it beside the birth chip.

### Styled
- **The aisle rows** (`src/components/org/variance-map.tsx`, `data-testid="cadence-door-aisles"`): the aisles the doors opened onto wear the door's violet house — a bordered block under the census line, one row per aisle (`cadence-door-aisle-{categoryId}`, aria-label speaking the aisle + its births), the aisle's name in violet, the count in `tabular-nums` ("1 sheet born here" / "N sheets born here"), and a slim share bar (`role="img"`, aria speaking the percent of the window's door births) with the same width transition the map's other bars wear. Dark halves from birth on every element; the echo chips (`sheet-drill-origin-aisle`, `stocktake-sheet-origin-aisle`) wear the exact violet family the birth chips speak — sibling spans, never rewrites (the chip law stays byte-identical).

## [2.241.0] The walk's law

The cadence has spoken the same pair since v2.235.0 — 7 fresh days, a 30-day window — but it spoke them as CONSTANTS, duplicated across two wires. This round the pair becomes the org's own: the owner writes the law once, and every voice that names a threshold (the org map's cadence block, the window figures, the coverage breakdown, the branch picker's teeth) reads the SAME stored pair. The law changes on the record, or not at all.

### Added
- **The law library** (`src/lib/cadence-law.ts`): the single home the duplicated constants become. `readCadenceLaw` reads the pair as ONE read (two keyed rows in SystemConfig: `cadence.freshDays`, `cadence.windowDays`), each key validated independently against the bands, the PAIR coherence-checked — and the FALLBACK LAW: an absent key, a malformed value, or an incoherent pair (freshDays >= windowDays) reads as the defaults 7/30, yesterday's truth, whole. The reader never throws into a request. THE BOUNDS: freshDays 1–14 (a week's shape), windowDays 14–90.
- **The law wire** (`GET/PUT /api/org/cadence`): GET returns the stored law + its bounds (manager-plus reads); PUT writes it as ONE pair from the owner's hand (SUPER_ADMIN / STORE_OWNER — a branch manager walks the floor, the owner writes the law). THE PAIR LAW: the body must be a coherent pair — half a law is rejected whole (400, the bands named in the error). THE AUDIT LAW: every write lands in the tamper-evident trail (CONFIG_CHANGE on SystemConfig/cadence, old pair and new pair hashed) — the law changes on the record.
- **The wires obey** (`/api/org/kpi-summary` + `/api/categories?includeCadence=true`): the duplicated constants are GONE — both wires read the law once per request, and the law's window governs the whole 30d-shaped family (the window figures, the cadence bands, the door-born census, the coverage). The payload rides the law's own numbers (`cadence.freshDays/windowDays`, additive `window30d.windowDays`) so an older cached answer keeps yesterday's silence (optional in the type, never assumed).
- **The law's face** (`src/components/org/variance-map.tsx`, `data-testid="cadence-law"`): the cadence block NAMES the pair ("the law: fresh ≤ 7d · window 30d — the pair every voice here speaks"), and the governor's hand grows the pen (`cadence-law-pen`, only for SUPER_ADMIN/STORE_OWNER — the FACE-BEFORE-CONTROL LAW: every reader sees the law named, only the governor sees the pen). The editor (`cadence-law-editor`) is two labelled number inputs with the client mirroring the wire's bands before the round trip; the wire's 400 body speaks into the error line verbatim (`cadence-law-error`, role=alert); a written law closes the editor, toasts the pair, and asks the page's own loader for the refetch (the map renders, it never re-sorts — the wire re-walks).

### Styled
- The law face rides the map's tile house: the Scale glyph as the law's aria-hidden glass, the muted 10px voice with `tabular-nums` on every figure, the amber focus ring on inputs and pens, the emerald verdict family on the save pen (disabled state while writing), the muted family on cancel, the red family on the error line — dark halves from birth on every element. THE WINDOW SPEAKS ITS NUMBER: every hardcoded "30-day"/"30 days" label in the map (the coverage tile's aria, the breakdown's note, the roster's cap note, the cadence bars' cap and aria, the cadence cap note) now says the law's own number — the labels are the wire's, never a constant.

## [2.240.0] The birth, everywhere

The yard's answer shipped the per-branch census (v2.239.0) — this round the birth is nameable at EVERY altitude of the map: roster rows wear the chip, and the branch face echoes it. One violet family, four altitudes: census → breakdown → roster → drill — and the branch's own glass.

### Added
- **The roster's birth** (`GET /api/org/kpi-summary`): the live-sheets and recent-sheets queries grow `origin` (one column each row already holds — zero new reads; the allowlist law means 'walk-door' or nothing), and the map's roster rows wear the chip (`data-testid="variance-map-live-origin-{id}"` on a live row, `data-testid="variance-map-row-origin-{id}"` on a roster row) — a door-born sheet is nameable BEFORE the drill opens. Additive + optional in the type so an older cached answer renders the silence.
- **The branch's echo** (`GET /api/stock-takes/[id]` + the branch sheet face): the branch detail wire grows the sheet's own `origin` (additive, one column the row already holds — zero new reads), and the branch face renders `data-testid="stocktake-sheet-origin"` beside the status chip — the same violet house, the DoorOpen glass, the law on the title. A hand-raised sheet wears nothing, both sides of the glass.

### Styled
- The BirthChip becomes a named face in the map (one component, one house — the drill's chip and the branch's echo wear the same classes): violet-50/700, ring-violet-200, dark 500/10+300, 9px semibold, `tabular-nums`, the aria-hidden DoorOpen glass. The live progress bars grow the width transition (duration-500) the breakdown's bars wear — one motion across the map. Dark halves from birth on every new face.

## [2.239.0] The yard's answer

The census shipped counting (v2.238.0) — this round it answers PER BRANCH: which yards actually answer their doors. The coverage breakdown grows the door-born chip; the drill's record face echoes a sheet's birth; and the census's faces name one violet family. The census counts doors, never guesses.

### Added
- **The yard's-answer wire** (`GET /api/org/kpi-summary` → `stockTakes.doorBornByStore`): one grouped read over the SAME window births the headline census already speaks (`origin='walk-door'`, `startedAt >= windowStart` — the same where-shape, zero new sheet queries), one row per store the census heard (`{ storeId, doorBorn }`; stores are five, never thousands — bounded by the catalog, not the ledger). Additive + optional in the type so an older cached answer renders the silent absence. A store ABSENT from the array answered nothing — the breakdown's silence is the honest zero, never a drawn one.
- **The yard's-answer chip** (`src/components/org/variance-map.tsx`, `data-testid="coverage-row-door-born-{storeId}"`): each breakdown row that ANSWERED wears the violet door chip — "{N} born at a door", the law on the title ("the org face pointed, this yard answered"). THE SILENCE LAW: the chip speaks only when the payload carries the census AND the count is > 0 — the org-level census already spoke the org's zero, so a zero branch wears the silence, never a drawn zero. The row's aria stays the coverage voice; the chip carries its own (one element, one voice).
- **The drill's birth chip** (`src/components/org/sheet-drill.tsx`, `data-testid="sheet-drill-origin"`): the org drill's wire grows the sheet's own `origin` (additive, one column the row already holds — zero new reads; the allowlist law means 'walk-door' or nothing), and a door-born sheet wears its birth beside the status chip: the DoorOpen glass + "born at a walk door", the law on the title. A hand-raised sheet wears nothing — the silence is the honest state.

### Styled
- The violet door family is born: the coverage chip and the drill's birth chip share one house (violet-50 / violet-700 / ring-violet-200, dark halves at the 500/10+300 family, 9px semibold, `tabular-nums` on the count) — distinct from the emerald books-heard, the amber pens, the red shorts. The census line wears the same DoorOpen glass (aria-hidden, decorative); the breakdown rows grow the hover surface (rounded-lg + slate-50/dark:slate-800/60 transition) and the bars gain the inner shadow track with a width transition; the toggle now names its flyout (`aria-controls="coverage-breakdown"`) and the flyout answers with its `id` — the disclosure pair complete. Dark halves from birth on every new face.

## [2.238.0] The door's census

The walk's doors shipped pointing (v2.237.0) — this round they can be COUNTED. A sheet born at a door carries its birth; the org's cadence speaks the window's census; and the coverage tile opens into the branch truth. The census counts doors, never guesses.

### Added
- **The origin column** (prisma: `stock_takes.origin`, nullable TEXT + migration): how a sheet was born. Additive + nullable — every existing sheet reads as hand-raised, which is exactly the truth. THE ALLOWLIST LAW (`POST /api/stock-takes`): the wire stores exactly ONE origin value (`walk-door`); anything else — a forged string, a stale client, a future caller — reads as a hand-raised sheet. A free-text origin would be a census anyone could edit; a closed vocabulary is a census the ledger can trust.
- **The birth rides the drawer** (`src/components/inventory/stock-take-panel.tsx`): `doorBorn` shares the whisper's life — set by the door's consume (a hit), silenced by the user's own hand (either scope button or the category select), cleared on success. Only a sheet whose AISLE the door actually aimed carries `origin: 'walk-door'` in its create payload — a hand-diverted drawer is a hand-raised sheet.
- **The census wire** (`GET /api/org/kpi-summary` → `stockTakes.doorBorn30d`): one bounded count — sheets BORN in the rolling 30-day window whose origin the create wire allowed. Births, not closes: a counting door-born sheet is still a walk answered. Additive + optional in the type so an older cached answer renders the silent absence (the face checks the type, never assumes).
- **The census face** (`src/components/org/variance-map.tsx`, `data-testid="cadence-door-census"`): beneath the cadence's tagline — "N sheets this window started at a walk door — the org face pointed, the floor answered." The census SPEAKS ITS ZERO honestly: a face may say nothing happened; only a CONTROL must hide.
- **The coverage breakdown** (`src/components/org/variance-map.tsx`): the coverage tile's label becomes a toggle (`data-testid="coverage-breakdown-toggle"`, aria-expanded, the ChevronDown turning) when the cadence speaks — a stale payload keeps the plain label. Open, it renders the branch truth beneath the window row (`data-testid="coverage-breakdown"`): one bar per branch (`data-testid="coverage-row-{storeId}"`), heard of total with the emerald books-heard fill, THE SILENT SHELVES LEADING (least heard first, heaviest roster as the tiebreak — the walk order's own spirit). Derived from the cadence rows the payload already carries — zero new reads: the rows the map renders ARE the rows the breakdown speaks.

### Styled
- The census line rides the cadence's muted voice at 10px with `tabular-nums` on the count; the breakdown toggle inherits the tile's uppercase label scale with the amber focus ring and the rotating chevron; the breakdown rows carry the map's tile house (rounded-xl, the emerald h-1 bars on the slate track, `tabular-nums` on every figure, the branch name truncated with its full voice on the aria). Dark halves from birth on every new face.

## [2.237.0] The walk's door

The cadence has said WHERE and WHEN since v2.235.0 — this round it grows HANDS. The org face's walk order becomes a set of doors: one click on a blind-spot aisle opens the branch's count sheet with that aisle already picked, the arrival whispered. And the walk order exports itself — one honest file, the floor's clipboard for the day. The org face points, the branch face counts, and the paper rides along.

### Added
- **The walk's door** (`src/components/org/variance-map.tsx`): every cadence row grows an anchor door (`data-testid="cadence-door-{categoryId}"`, "walk" + the ChevronRight glyph) to `/stores/{slug}/inventory?countSheet={categoryId}` — THE ANCHOR LAW (v2.232.0's, verbatim): an anchor, never a button, so the middle-click and the keyboard both keep their rights. THE SLUG LAW: the door rides the store's OWN slug from the stores prop (the same truth the branch selector speaks) — a slug-less store holds no door, because a control that cannot navigate never lies. THE ZERO-FETCH LAW: the door composes the cadence payload's own `storeId` + the stores prop's slug — no wire grew, no read was added.
- **The branch's arrival** (`src/components/inventory/stock-take-panel.tsx`): the panel reads `?countSheet={categoryId}` ONE time at mount (the door is a hard anchor navigation — the panel mounts fresh WITH the query in the URL). THE GATE LAW: the categories wire only speaks when the drawer is open on the category scope, so the consume opens the drawer first and reads when the wire lands — two passes, one ref, one-shot by construction. THE OWN-SHELF LAW: the aisle is pre-picked only if THIS store's categories wire carries it — a foreign id is discarded and the face quietly returns to its own state; the picker never lies. THE QUIET-URL LAW: the query is stripped after the consume (history.replaceState) — a refresh never re-haunts the drawer. The arrival wears its whisper (`data-testid="stocktake-door-prefill"` — "the org's walk order sent you here — {aisle} · {days voice}."), riding the muted family; the user's own hand (either scope button or the category select) silences it, because a provenance line that outlived its moment would lie.
- **The walk's paper** (`src/components/org/variance-map.tsx`): the cadence block exports itself — a "walk order" button (`data-testid="cadence-export"`, the Download glyph) over the PURE `cadenceWalkCsv(rows)` helper. Six columns: aisle, store, band, days_since, last_counted_at, skus — in the WIRE'S OWN ORDER (the face renders, the file speaks, neither re-sorts). The drill's CSV laws ride verbatim: `csvCell`'s quoting, the BOM so Excel opens UTF-8 honestly, CRLF joins, and the honest absences export as EMPTY cells (a never-heard shelf has no date; the file never invents one). The aria names the whole-order law ("Download all N aisles as CSV — the walk order as the wire speaks it, blind spots first"); the button hides on a shelf-less cadence — an absent control never lies.

### Styled
- The door is the map's own door language at the pens' scale: the 9px semibold slate outline family with the amber focus ring, the hover lifting to the row's own surface (slate-50 / dark:slate-800), `self-center` so a long aisle name never bends it. The whisper rides the cadence's muted voice (italic slate-500 / dark:slate-400 at 9px, the History glyph as the cadence's signature); the export button wears the house border family with the decorative Download glass — distinct from the amber pens, the emerald verdict, the violet hand: a document affordance, not a state.

## [2.236.0] The teeth

The cadence's teeth. The org face has pointed since v2.235.0 — this round the branch picker listens: the stocktake create dialog's category picker wears the same four bands, so the walker knows WHICH aisles are stale before the walk starts. And the map's window row learns to answer "how much of the org did we actually hear?" — the coverage tile, one subtraction, zero new reads.

### Added
- **The teeth wire** (`GET /api/categories` → opt-in `includeCadence=true`): per category of THIS store, the same four bands the org map speaks — **fresh** (heard ≤ 7 days), **stale** (within the 30-day window, colder), **beyond** (counted before, not inside this window), **never** (no closed line has EVER touched it) — plus `daysSince`, `lastCountedAt`, and the thresholds the face speaks (`freshDays`, `windowDays`). THE STORE LAW: the reads ride the wire's own `storeId` — a branch hears its own shelves. THE OPT-IN LAW: the field rides only when asked — every other consumer of this wire reads the exact bytes it read yesterday. THE COST LAW: no cost field crosses the block — the cadence reads the catalog, not the caller's gate. THE VOICE-NOT-SORT LAW: the rows keep the categories' own order; the walk order belongs to the org map.
- **The picker's cadence voice** (the stocktake create dialog): every option label speaks the days voice ("Cement · 4 products · never counted"); the legend (`data-testid="stocktake-cadence-legend"`) names the thresholds from the wire's own numbers, never a constant; and the picked aisle wears the strip (`data-testid="stocktake-pick-cadence"`) — the band chip, the days voice, and the heat bar under the org face's own length law (capped at the window, 4% floor, the dashed track reserved for never).
- **The coverage tile** (the variance map's window row): a FIFTH tile — "Heard · 30d" (`data-testid="variance-map-coverage"`) — the share of branch categories the window heard (fresh + stale of total) from the cadence counts the payload already carries: zero new reads, zero new fetches. The honest absences speak: an older cached answer says "the cadence has not spoken yet"; an org with no categories says "no shelves to hear"; the dashed track is the absence's track, never a drawn zero.

### Styled
- The picker's strip inherits the cadence's whole house at the pens' own scale: the four band families copied verbatim (fresh emerald, stale amber, beyond orange at 70%, never the muted slate), the 9px semibold uppercase chip, the `h-1 rounded-full` bar on the same slate track, `tabular-nums` on every figure, dark halves from birth, and the aria naming the aisle, the voice, and the weight. The coverage tile rides the map's tile house (`rounded-xl`, the 10px uppercase label, the bold tabular figure) and the cadence's bar language — the emerald books-heard fill, the dashed track when the cadence has not spoken.

## [2.235.0] The cadence

The map has said WHAT walked since v2.220.0 — this round it says WHEN. The variance map grows the cadence: per branch category, the days since a CLOSED count sheet last touched its shelf, in the walk order the floor should follow — blind spots first. Three bounded grouped reads compose it on the wire the console already polls; the face renders, it never re-sorts, and an empty shelf is a real shelf (a category with zero products still gets its row).

### Added
- **The cadence wire** (`GET /api/org/kpi-summary` → `stockTakes.cadence`): an additive block composed of three bounded reads — the window's line pairs (bounded by the closed-30d sheets the headline figures already speak; their read grows `closedAt` and stays ONE read for two consumers), the heard-ever product set (one `groupBy` over closed sheets, one row per distinct counted product), and the active catalog categories (tens, never thousands). A line reaches its category through its product — lines carry no categoryId of their own, so the product→category map is the only bridge, and the composition says so. The four voices: **fresh** (heard ≤ 7 days), **stale** (heard in the window, colder than 7), **beyond** (counted before, but not inside this window), **never** (no closed line has EVER touched it — the honest blind spot, never dressed as a date). No cost field crosses this wire: the cadence reads the catalog, not the caller's gate — every org role hears WHEN a shelf was last heard.
- **The walk order** (the wire sorts, the face obeys): never first (the blind spots, heaviest shelf first — `productCount` desc), then beyond, then stale (most neglected first — `daysSince` desc), then fresh (least fresh first). The face renders the wire's order; it never re-sorts — one sorting law, spoken once.
- **The cadence face** (`src/components/org/variance-map.tsx`): beneath the recent-sheets roster (`data-testid="cadence-block"`), the History icon names the section, the four band chips count each voice (`data-testid="cadence-chip-fresh|stale|beyond|never"` — a zero band wears the muted house at 60% opacity instead of vanishing), and every category row (`data-testid="cadence-row-{categoryId}"`) carries a colored left rail, the band chip (`data-testid="cadence-band-{categoryId}"`), the days voice (`data-testid="cadence-days-{categoryId}"` — "counted today" / "1 day ago" / "N days ago" / "last heard beyond the window" / "never heard"), its SKU weight, and the heat bar — length ALWAYS means time since last heard (daysSince capped at the 30-day window, 4% floor so a fresh shelf still shows a sliver), fresh emerald, stale amber, beyond orange, never a dashed empty track. The honest step-aside (`data-testid="cadence-stale"`) renders when an older cached answer predates the block, and the cap note (`data-testid="cadence-cap-note"`) speaks the window law: a shelf absent from it has not been heard in 30 days; a never-counted shelf has no closed line at all.

### Styled
- The cadence face inherits the map's whole house: `rounded-2xl` panel, `rounded-lg` rows with the tinted left rail (`w-1` — emerald/amber/orange/slate by band, dark halves from birth), `tabular-nums` on every count and days figure, the muted family (slate-500 / dark:slate-400) on every sub-line, the 9px semibold chip scale the status chips speak. The heat bar rides the same `h-1 rounded-full` track the live counts use — one bar language across the map — with the dashed variant reserved for never (an absence is never drawn as a value). Every row carries an aria naming the category, its store, the days voice, and its SKU weight; the band chips are the same ring-1 house at the same 9px.

## [2.234.0] The verdict

The lineage reads backwards. v2.233.0 taught the faces to read a sheet's note — a child knows its parent ("recount of sheet …"). This round the parent learns its child: both detail wires grow one bounded read over the spawn's own notes signature, and the faces wear the verdict — who re-walked this sheet, what the re-walk found, and (on the org drill) a door that re-keys the read onto the child. The loop now speaks in both directions.

### Added
- **The verdict wire** (`GET /api/org/stock-takes/[id]` + `GET /api/stock-takes/[id]`): an additive `recountedBy` block — one `findFirst` over the notes the spawn signs (`recount of sheet {id}`, the exact string or with a trailing space: the clause stays exact), latest child first, the child's variance units riding the SAME read (its lines come with it, bounded by the child's own line count). Units are units: the number only speaks once the child is CLOSED (a counting child has not spoken; a voided child never will). The cost gate is NOT involved — this is the units law, not the shillings law. Null when no child exists (the honest absence).
- **The verdict face** (`src/components/org/sheet-drill.tsx`): a sheet with a re-walk grows the emerald record face (`data-testid="sheet-drill-verdict"`) under its lineage/notes faces — the child's FULL id, its units once closed, and the status voice (closed + zero = "the re-walk found the shelf true — the dispute settled at zero."; closed + N = "the re-walk spoke — N units between book and shelf."; counting = "the re-walk is on the floor — the verdict waits."; cancelled = "the re-walk was voided before it spoke."). The face rides the paper; its control does not.
- **The verdict door** (`sheet-drill.tsx` + `variance-map.tsx`): the face carries one BUTTON (`data-testid="sheet-drill-verdict-open"`, "open the re-walk", `data-sheet-print-off` — a control is a screen thing) that re-keys the map's drill onto the child via the new optional `onOpenSheet` prop — a fresh drill by the map's own door law (the per-sheet key remounts, the instruments reset by construction), one deliberate fetch for the sheet the user OPENED. NAVIGATION, never a pen: both faces stay READ-ONLY BY LAW. The same store by construction (the spawn posts to the branch's own wire), so the parent's storeName rides.
- **The branch verdict line** (`src/components/inventory/stock-take-panel.tsx`): the walker opening an older disputed sheet sees the verdict under the header (`data-testid="stocktake-recounted-by"` — the child's full id + the same voice family, emerald, riding the paper). A LINE, never a nav control — the roster's own View button already reaches the child.

### Styled
- The verdict face is the books-heard-it emerald (border + wash both halves from birth: emerald-200/emerald-50/60/emerald-800 light, emerald-500/40 + emerald-500/10 + emerald-200 dark), the voice at 9px in emerald-700 / emerald-300/80, `tabular-nums` on every id and units figure; the open-child button is the 9px emerald outline family with the ArrowRight glyph and an aria naming the child it opens; the branch line wears the same emerald at the pens' own scale. The verdict face sits BENEATH the v2.233.0 lineage faces — a chained recount (a sheet with both a parent and a child) reads amber above emerald, ancestry above verdict, in one glance.

## [2.233.0] The lineage

A sheet's note has always been a record — this round the faces learn to READ it. The v2.232.0 recount signs its sheets `recount of sheet {id}`; the cancel path keeps the branch's reason. Until now both arrived as raw text the UI merely echoed (or, on the org map's rosters, hid entirely). v2.233.0 teaches the faces to parse the note and wear it: the lineage chip on three rosters, the why-line under a cancelled row, the full-record faces in the drills. Zero new fetches — the notes already rode every wire.

### Added
- **The lineage reader** (`src/components/org/primitives.tsx`): a PURE parser — `parseSheetLineage(notes)` returns `{ kind: 'recount', sourceSheetId, remainder }` for a note matching `recount of sheet <id>` (case-insensitive, id `[\w-]+`, any remainder rides verbatim), `{ kind: 'note', text }` for any other non-empty note, and `null` for null/empty/whitespace (the honest absence — no note, no chip: an absent control never lies). Beside it `lineageShortId` (the roster whisper: `…` + last six of the id) and the shared `LineageChip` (one house style for every face: the amber family, tabular id, the FULL id on both `title` and `aria-label` — the eye gets the whisper, the ear gets the record).
- **The org map's rosters wear the lineage** (`src/components/org/variance-map.tsx`): the recent-sheets roster renders the amber chip beside the status of any sheet whose note speaks a recount (`data-testid="variance-map-lineage-{id}"`), and a CANCELLED sheet with a reason grows its own why-line (`data-testid="variance-map-why-{id}"` — "why: {reason}", italic muted family, truncated with the full text on the hover). The live-counts row upgrades too: a recount lineage replaces the raw-text murmur with the chip; a plain note keeps the verbatim text it always had. Zero new fetches — the kpi wire has carried `notes` since the map was born.
- **The drill's record faces** (`src/components/org/sheet-drill.tsx`): the notes block becomes three faces under one law (the note speaks verbatim; the face only decides HOW LOUD): a recount lineage wears the amber record face (`data-testid="sheet-drill-lineage"` — the FULL source id, any remainder, and a status-voiced line: counting = "the re-walk is on the floor — these lines were the last dispute.", closed = "the dispute was re-walked — this sheet is the verdict.", cancelled = "the re-walk was voided before it spoke."); a CANCELLED sheet's note earns the "why the void" face (`data-testid="sheet-drill-void-why"`); any other note keeps the verbatim box it always had (`data-testid="sheet-drill-notes"`, untouched). All three ride the paper — a printed record carries its own lineage.
- **The branch face wears it too** (`src/components/inventory/stock-take-panel.tsx`): the roster row upgrades (chip replaces the raw recount text; plain notes unchanged), the cancelled face names its reason (`data-testid="stocktake-cancelled-why"` — "why: {reason}"), and the walker opening a recount sheet sees the lineage under the header (`data-testid="stocktake-sheet-lineage"` — the full source id where the pens live, riding the paper).

### Styled
- The lineage chip is the sheet's amber house: `bg-amber-50`/`text-amber-800`/`ring-amber-200` light, `bg-amber-500/10`/`text-amber-200`/`ring-amber-500/30` dark (dark halves from birth), `tabular-nums` on the id, 9px semibold — the same scale the status chips speak, so a row reads status → lineage → name in one breath. The why-lines wear the muted family (slate-500 / dark:slate-400) in italic at 10px with a hairline top border on the map's roster (the row's own divider), truncated with the full text on the hover; the drill's record face keeps the clipboard-note's amber family (border + wash both halves) with the status voice at 9px in amber-700 / amber-300/80. Every new face passes the 3:1 chip bar; every control-free face carries no print-off mark — the paper is the record, lineage included.

## [2.232.0] The recount

The loop closes. A CLOSED sheet's variances have always been a verdict — this round they become the NEXT sheet's population: one button freezes a fresh count scoped to exactly the disputed lines, and the walk verifies what the last walk disputed. The third reading lens grows on both faces; the org drill grows a door that points at the branch.

### Added
- **The variances-only lens** (`src/components/inventory/stock-take-panel.tsx` + `src/components/org/sheet-drill.tsx`): the third instrument beside the category lens and the name/SKU finder — an aria-pressed chip (`data-testid="stocktake-variances-only"` / `data-testid="sheet-drill-variances-only"`, label "variances only · N") that narrows the reading to the lines whose counted ≠ snapshot. It composes with the other two needles over the SAME held lines in one clause; the honest N-of-M count answers all three; the empty face grows the honest variants (shelf + variances, variances alone — impossible by construction since the chip mounts only when the sheet holds at least one variance line, but the fallback stays honest). The law of every instrument holds: the reading narrows, never the sheet — the progress bar, the Print aria, the tiles and the CSV keep the whole-sheet truth. Per-door law: the chip is STATE of the door; every door-opening resets it beside the lens and the needle.
- **The recount spawn** (`stock-take-panel.tsx`, the pens' face): a CLOSED face holding at least one variance line grows the recount zone (`data-testid="stocktake-recount-zone"`, amber dashed, `data-sheet-print-off`): one button (`data-testid="stocktake-recount"`, "Recount the variances — freeze N products") POSTs the create wire's explicit-list scope — `productIds` = the variance lines' unique product ids, `notes: "recount of sheet {id}"` — re-validated server-side against THIS store's active products (the empty-population 400 stays honest). Success opens the fresh door in the SAME panel under the fresh-door law (instruments reset, roster and tab stock re-read): the floor keeps walking, and the new sheet's notes name the sheet being re-walked.
- **The one-open-sheet law keeps its teeth**: while the roster holds a COUNTING sheet, the zone speaks honestly (`data-testid="stocktake-recount-blocked"` — "a sheet is already counting — the recount waits for the open sheet to close or cancel") and the button is ABSENT (an absent control never lies); the wire's 409 stays the backstop and surfaces as the toast, never swallowed.
- **The recount door** (`src/components/org/sheet-drill.tsx` + `GET /api/org/stock-takes/[id]`): the drill wire's store select grows the additive `slug` (one cheap column, catalog-bounded, zero new fetches — the address rides the fetch the drill already makes), and the drill's CLOSED face grows an ANCHOR (`data-testid="sheet-drill-recount-door"` — never a button: this face stays READ-ONLY BY LAW, the branch face keeps the only pens) linking to `/stores/{slug}/inventory`, where the recount spawn lives. The address is the wire's OWN slug, never a client-side guess; a store without a slug holds no door (an absent control never lies). The door stays off the paper.

### Styled
- The variances-only chip wears each face's own scale (h-7 at branch, h-6 at org) in the sheet's amber house: active = amber-700/white with the amber ring, idle = the slate ring family with the amber focus ring and honest hover halves; dark halves from birth; `tabular-nums` on the count. The recount zone is the floor-copy note's family (amber dashed border, amber-50/60 light, amber-500/10 dark) with an amber-700 button carrying the RotateCcw glyph and the honest pending spin; the drill's door is the same family with an ArrowRight glyph and a muted sub-line naming the spawn's scope; every new control carries `data-sheet-print-off` — the paper is the sheet, never its instruments.

## [2.231.0] The branch lens

The walk reads its shelves. The v2.230.0 lens taught the org drill to narrow a sheet by category; this round the branch stocktake face — the panel the floor actually walks — grows the same instruments: a category lens AND the name/SKU finder the drill has kept since v2.223.0, composed over the lines the face already holds. The reading narrows; the sheet never does.

### Added
- **The line names its shelf, at branch level** (`GET /api/stock-takes/[id]`): the branch detail wire's per-line product select grows the same additive `category` join the org drill's wire grew in v2.230.0 (the product's own `ProductCategory` name, `null` when the product never joined one). One query, zero new fetches — the lens's raw material rides the fetch the panel already makes. The client type stays optional (`category?: string | null`), the same law the drill's line type reads.
- **The walk's instruments** (`src/components/inventory/stock-take-panel.tsx`): the sheet face grows a controls row (`data-sheet-print-off` — controls stay off the paper) between the header card and the lines: a category `<select>` (`data-testid="stocktake-lens"`, aria "Filter the lines by category") whose options are the sheet's OWN buckets — named shelves alphabetical, `uncategorized` a real bucket sorted last — mounted only when the sheet spans MORE THAN ONE bucket (an absent control never lies); a name/SKU finder (`data-testid="stocktake-finder"` with its clear button) the branch face never had; the honest N-of-M count (`data-testid="stocktake-finder-count"`); and the amber shelf name (`data-testid="stocktake-lens-count"`, "in Iron Sheets") when a shelf is lensed. The law of the instruments is the law of the drill: they narrow the READING, never the sheet — the progress bar and summary keep the whole-sheet truth, the Print aria names the whole count, and the CSV keeps the whole file (seven columns, unchanged).
- **The composed reading** (`stock-take-panel.tsx`): lens and needle filter the SAME held lines in one clause; the lines list renders the narrowed reading while every printed/exported byte stays whole-sheet. The empty face grows the drill's honest variants (`data-testid="stocktake-finder-empty"`): needle only, shelf + needle, or shelf alone (impossible by construction — buckets derive FROM the lines — but the fallback stays honest).
- **The fresh-door law** (`stock-take-panel.tsx`): the instruments are STATE of the door, not of the sheet — every door-opening (roster View/Resume, a fresh create, the back door) resets the lens to "all categories" and the needle to empty by construction, never by effect. A stale reading can't leak into the next sheet.
- **The shelf whisper, on the walk** (`stock-take-panel.tsx`): while the sheet spans buckets, every line wears its category (`data-testid="stocktake-line-shelf"`, 9px uppercase tracking-wide in the muted slate family) at the head of its right cluster — and the whisper PRINTS (no print-off mark): a printed walk reads its shelves, the clipboard's count boxes and the shelf names on one paper.

### Styled
- The lens and finder wear the drill's house: h-7 compact controls (the branch face's own scale), the slate border family with the AMBER focus ring (the sheet's hue), the finder's inset search glyph and `placeholder` in the muted family, `max-w-[10rem]` on the lens so a long shelf name truncates instead of shoving the row; the lens-count span the amber family at weight 600 with tabular-nums; the empty face the house dashed-border family; the line's right cluster grows `flex-wrap justify-end` so whisper + chip + cost + controls lay out cleanly on narrow screens and on paper; dark halves from birth on every new element.

## [2.230.0] The sheet's lens

The drill learns to read by shelf. The v2.223.0 line finder narrowed the reading by name and SKU; this round the drill grows its twin — a category lens over the same held lines, backed by an additive wire growth that lets every line speak its shelf.

### Added
- **The line names its shelf** (`GET /api/org/stock-takes/[id]`): the drill wire's per-line product select grows an additive `category` join (the product's own `ProductCategory` name, `null` when the product never joined one). One query, zero new fetches — the lens's raw material rides the fetch the drill already makes. The line type on the client is optional (`category?: string | null`) so the branch panel's own `SheetLine` — the other caller of the exported `sheetLinesCsv` — stays assignable untouched.
- **The sheet's lens** (`src/components/org/sheet-drill.tsx`): the drill's controls row grows a category `<select>` (`data-testid="sheet-drill-lens"`, aria "Filter the lines by category") beside the finder. Its options are the sheet's OWN buckets, derived client-side from the lines the drill already holds — named categories alphabetical, and `uncategorized` a real bucket that sorts last (named shelves first). The law of the lens is the law of the finder: it narrows the READING, never the sheet — the summary tiles keep the whole sheet's arithmetic, the CSV keeps the whole sheet's file (seven columns, unchanged), and the count line stays an honest N of M that answers to BOTH instruments. The lens mounts only when the sheet spans MORE THAN ONE bucket — a one-shelf sheet has nothing to lens (an absent control never lies). Like the needle, the lens is STATE of the drill, not of the sheet: the map keys the drill per sheet, so a fresh door opens at "all categories" by construction.
- **The combined reading** (`sheet-drill.tsx`): lens and needle compose — the lens narrows the shelf, the needle narrows the line, and one filter answers to both. When a shelf is lensed, a separate amber span (`data-testid="sheet-drill-lens-count"`) names it beside the count ("in Iron Sheets"); the empty face grows honest variants — needle only, shelf + needle, or shelf alone (impossible by construction, but the fallback stays honest).
- **The shelf whisper** (`sheet-drill.tsx`): while the sheet spans more than one bucket, every line wears its category (`data-testid="sheet-drill-line-category"`, 9px uppercase in the muted family) beside its snapshot arithmetic — context for the lens on screen AND for the paper: the whisper carries NO print-off mark, so a printed walk reads its shelves.

### Styled
- The lens select wears the finder's house: h-6, 10px, the slate border family with the AMBER focus ring (the sheet's hue), `max-w-[10rem]` so a long category name truncates instead of shoving the row; dark halves from birth. The lens-count span is the amber family at weight 600 with tabular-nums; the shelf whisper is 9px uppercase tracking-wide in the muted slate family; every new element carries its dark half and the print vocabulary (`data-sheet-print-off` on the instruments, nothing on the whisper).

## [2.229.0] The branch walk

The clipboard reaches the branch — the face where the floor actually walks. The v2.228.0 clipboard made a counting sheet's paper a TOOL on the org drill; this round the branch stocktake face (the panel the floor opens, counts in, closes) prints the same walk-list under the same law. And the sheet's file reaches the branch too: the CSV the org ledger keeps, the branch keeps as well — one builder, never re-invented.

### Added
- **The branch walk** (`src/app/globals.css` + `src/components/inventory/stock-take-panel.tsx` + `src/components/org/sheet-drill.tsx`): while the branch sheet face is open, the body carries the SAME `sheet-printing` class the org drill reads (a mount/unmount lifecycle effect keyed on a boolean derivation — hooks above the early return, the class lives exactly as long as the face does, stable across refetches). The panel's card wears the drill's own vocabulary — `data-sheet-print-overlay` / `data-sheet-print` / `data-sheet-print-off` / `data-sheet-clipboard` — and the print stylesheet lays the sheet on the page: header, progress, lines with their variance chips. While the sheet still COUNTS, its paper grows the clipboard's three print-only fittings: the floor-copy note ("floor copy — walk the shelves, write what you see; this app holds the pens when you're back"), an EMPTY COUNT BOX on every line (a line the system already heard still walks — the walk IS the verification), and the signature rules at the foot (counted by / checked by / date). CLOSED and CANCELLED sheets keep the record face — chips, not boxes; the paper's mode IS the sheet's status, never a toggle. The controls (back, Print, CSV, count inputs, record buttons, the close and cancel zones, the result faces) stay off the paper. The print CSS grows ADDITIVE selectors keyed on a shared `data-sheet-lines` mark (both faces wear it) — one scroll-clamp release and one `break-inside: avoid` law serve every sheet's paper, and the drill's own testid-keyed rules stay untouched.
- **The paper names its branch** (`GET /api/stock-takes/[id]` + panel): the detail wire grows an additive `store` block (id / name / code — the same shape the org drill's wire speaks), and the sheet's header line wears the branch's own name (`data-testid="stocktake-store-name"`). A ring binder holds five branches' walks — a sheet that doesn't say which yard walked it is an ambiguous paper. Zero new fetches; the name rode along.
- **The sheet's doors at branch level** (`stock-take-panel.tsx`): the header card grows the Print button (`data-testid="stocktake-print"`, aria mode-conditional — "Print the floor's clipboard — N lines with empty count boxes" while COUNTING, "Print all N lines of the count sheet at {branch}" on the record face) and the CSV button (`data-testid="stocktake-export"`, aria "Download all N lines as CSV"), both mounted under the SAME gate (a sheet that froze no lines prints and exports nothing but silence — an absent control never lies). The file rides the SAME pure `sheetLinesCsv` the org drill exports — BOM, CRLF, the seven columns, cost-gated cells export EMPTY (the branch's gate is the panel's own `canViewCost` prop); imported, never re-invented, so a branch's file reads exactly like the org ledger's file.

### Styled
- The branch Print and CSV buttons wear the drill's house border family with the AMBER focus ring (the sheet's hue); the floor-copy note wears the amber dashed family; the count box rides a dashed hairline in the slate family on paper (print-color-adjust: exact inherited); the signature rules are honest hairlines with uppercase 9px labels; the branch name on the header line is the slate-700/dark:slate-200 emphasis half of the muted meta line; dark halves from birth on every new element; tabular-nums everywhere a figure rides.

## [2.228.0] The clipboard

The paper becomes the floor's tool. The v2.227.0 sheet's paper laid the count on the page as a RECORD; this round a still-counting sheet prints as a WALK-LIST — every line gets an empty count box for the pen, the paper names itself a floor copy, and the foot carries signature rules. And the procurement ledger's watchlist whisper learns to speak its names: the amber chip opens the speakers themselves, each with the numbers the branch's own watchlist reads.

### Added
- **The clipboard** (`src/app/globals.css` + `src/components/org/sheet-drill.tsx`): while a sheet's status is COUNTING, its print face grows three print-only fittings — a floor-copy note under the header ("floor copy — walk the shelves, write what you see; the branch app holds the pens"), an EMPTY COUNT BOX on every line (a ruled write-in spanning the line's width on paper; a line the system already heard still gets its box, because the walk is the verification), and the signature rules at the foot (counted by / checked by / date — a walk needs its witnesses). The base stylesheet keeps all three off the screen (a screen face never pretends to be a pen); the print stylesheet lays them on the paper; each line rides with `break-inside: avoid` so a line and its box never split across pages. CLOSED and CANCELLED sheets keep the v2.227 record face — chips, not boxes: the paper answers to the sheet's status, never to a toggle. The Print button's aria speaks the mode ("Print the floor's clipboard — N lines with empty count boxes").
- **The whisper speaks its names** (`GET /api/org/purchase-orders` + `src/components/org/po-ledger.tsx`): the watchlist stitch's per-(store, supplier) hint now carries its first THREE speakers beside the count — name + stock / level pairs, the SAME numbers the branch's own watchlist face reads (the org sees the branch's truth, never a second derivation). The candidates query grew one cheap column (name — catalog-bounded, not history-bounded); zero new fetches, zero new queries. The amber chip is now a door of its own (a Radix popover trigger OUTSIDE the drill button — a door inside a door is a lie the DOM refuses): clicking it opens a read-only popover listing the speakers ("N on the shelf / N floor" per row), the count line names the store and supplier, a "+K more on the branch's own watchlist face" line discloses the cap, and the footer names the supplier-of-record law. Zero stays silent — a row with no hints carries no trigger; absence stays absent.

### Styled
- The count box rides a dashed hairline in the slate family on paper (print-color-adjust: exact inherited from the sheet's card); the floor-copy note wears the amber family's dashed border; the signature rules are honest hairlines with uppercase 9px labels; the watchlist trigger gains an alert glyph and a hover half (amber-100 light, amber-500/20 dark) with the amber focus ring, and the popover is the amber family at 3:1 on both halves (amber-50/amber-800 light, slate-900 card with amber-200 text dark) with white speaker cards inside — dark halves from birth; tabular-nums on every figure.

## [2.227.0] The sheet's paper

The ring binder's paper reaches the second shelf. The v2.224.0 sheet drill grew the count's file (CSV); this round the sheet grows its PAPER — while a drill is open, printing lays the count itself on the page. And the procurement ledger's rows learn to whisper: each row wears its branch's watchlist count against the row's own supplier, the floor hinting at the order before anyone raises it.

### Added
- **The sheet's print face** (`src/app/globals.css` + `src/components/org/sheet-drill.tsx`): while a count-sheet drill is open, the body carries the `sheet-printing` class (a mount/unmount lifecycle effect riding ABOVE the null guard — hooks never ride a conditional), and the print stylesheet silences the shell and lays the open sheet on the page: header, summary tiles, notes, lines with their variance chips — chips keep their colors (`print-color-adjust: exact`). The controls (close, finder, CSV/Print buttons, the N-of-M count) stay off the paper via `data-sheet-print-off` — a control is a screen thing. Without an open drill the class is absent and printing behaves exactly as before; the v2.226 PO paper is untouched (the two faces are mutually exclusive by construction — one drill door at a time).
- **The sheet's Print button** (`sheet-drill.tsx`): named for the ear (`data-testid="sheet-drill-print"`, aria-label "Print all N lines of the count sheet at …", title speaking the law); mounts beside the CSV under the SAME gate — a sheet that froze no lines prints nothing but silence. `window.print()` is the whole machinery; the stylesheet does the work.
- **The watchlist stitch** (`GET /api/org/purchase-orders` + `src/components/org/po-ledger.tsx`): the ledger wire now answers, per row, `watchlistHints` — how many products at or below their reorder level at the row's OWN store last spoke to the row's supplier (the branch low-stock wire's own supplier-of-record derivation: the most recent PO that carried the product wins). Two bounded queries ride inside the same `runWithoutTenant` (candidates: the active products of the window's stores, three cheap columns; items: PO history of the low candidates only) and the stitch answers for the narrowing — a branch filter narrows the candidates. The row wears the count on an amber chip (the watchlist's own family) with the whole sentence on its title; zero stays silent — absence stays absent, never a padded chip. The roster CSV keeps its seven columns: the stitch is a live whisper, not a row of the file.

### Styled
- The Print button wears the sheet drill's house border family with the AMBER focus ring (the sheet's hue — the PO drill's print button keeps its sky); the watchlist chip is the amber family at 3:1 on both halves (amber-50/amber-700 light, amber-500/10/amber-300 dark, amber ring), tabular-nums on the count; the paper face inherits the sheet's tiles, notes and variance chips with exact color reproduction; dark halves from birth on every new element.

## [2.226.0] The paper trail

The ring binder gets its paper. The v2.225.0 drill grew the order's file (CSV); this round the order grows its SHEET — while a drill is open, printing lays the document itself on the page, and the lines carry the voices they always held in the database (their notes). And the ledger's window joins the portable family: one file for the whole roster.

### Added
- **The print face** (`src/app/globals.css` + `src/components/org/po-drill.tsx`): while a PO drill is open, the body carries the `po-printing` class (a mount/unmount lifecycle effect — the class lives exactly as long as the door does), and the print stylesheet silences the shell and lays the open document on the page: header, summary tiles, timeline, lines with their received progress — chips and bars keep their colors (`print-color-adjust: exact`). The controls (close, finder, CSV/Print buttons, the N-of-M count) stay off the paper via `data-po-print-off` — a control is a screen thing. Without an open drill the class is absent and printing behaves exactly as before (the v2.152 console header/footer rule untouched).
- **The Print button** (`po-drill.tsx`): names the act for the ear and the eye (`data-testid="po-drill-print"`, aria-label "Print purchase order …", title speaking the law); it mounts only once the document has loaded — an absent control never lies. `window.print()` is the whole machinery; the stylesheet does the work.
- **The line's note** (`GET /api/org/purchase-orders/[id]` + `po-drill.tsx`): the drill wire now selects `items.notes` and every line carries it; a line with a note speaks it under its name on a quotation hairline (italic, muted family) — someone's voice on the record, not a system field. A line without one stays quiet: absence stays absent, on screen and on paper alike.
- **The roster's copy** (`src/components/org/po-ledger.tsx`): the ledger grows a CSV export over the rows it ALREADY holds (zero new fetch) — the WHOLE WINDOW the wire answered by law (the finder narrows the reading, never the export; the aria names it). Seven columns (po_number, status, supplier, branch, lines, ordered_day, total_kes); a cost-gated cell exports EMPTY (absence stays absence); the BOM rides; CRLF joins; the boring quoting law reused verbatim from the sheet drill's pure `csvCell`. File name carries the day: `po-ledger-YYYY-MM-DD.csv`.

### Styled
- The Print button wears the drill's house border family with the sky focus ring (a sibling of the CSV button it sits beside); the note rides a left quotation hairline in the muted family at 3:1, italic — a voice, not a field; the paper face inherits the document's ladder chips and received-progress bars with exact color reproduction; dark halves from birth on every new element; tabular-nums on every figure.

## [2.225.0] The pipeline from above

The procurement pipeline joins the org shelf. The v2.224.0 hint chips taught the org to read WHICH purchase order spoke to a restock row — but the documents themselves lived only in the branch workspaces. An org role could see the hints' receipts and never the pipeline. This round the console reads the pipeline: every branch's purchase orders, newest first, with honest counts on every status rung — and each row is a door into the document's lines, its received progress, and its timeline.

### Added
- **The procurement ledger** (`GET /api/org/purchase-orders` + `src/components/org/po-ledger.tsx`, mounted on the console beside the variance map): the org's PO roster — every branch's orders with the FULL status ladder (DRAFT → PENDING_APPROVAL → APPROVED → SENT → CONFIRMED → PARTIALLY_RECEIVED → RECEIVED / CANCELLED) as filter chips carrying REAL counts (a `groupBy` over the same org-wide where), a branch picker that narrows by refetch (the counts answer for the narrowing too), and a client-side finder over the loaded window with the honest N-of-M. The take is clamped (1..100, default 30) and DISCLOSED — when the pipe runs deeper than the window, the face says so. An out-of-ladder status filter is REFUSED (400), never silently "all".
- **The PO drill** (`GET /api/org/purchase-orders/[id]` + `src/components/org/po-drill.tsx`): a roster row opens ONE purchase order's lines org-side — ordered vs received units per line with a received-progress hairline, the raised→approved→received/cancelled timeline with names and times, notes, and the order's expected day. Every status drills: a DRAFT shows its ink, a RECEIVED its fulfillment, a CANCELLED its grave. READ-ONLY BY LAW — the branch workspace keeps the only pens; the read runs inside `runWithoutTenant` on the raw client (an org read is org-wide by definition; the v2.57.0 leak class must never shrink it).
- **The order's copy** (`po-drill.tsx`): the sheet drill's v2.224.0 law grown to the pipeline — a client-side CSV over the lines the drill ALREADY holds (zero new fetch). Seven columns (product, sku, unit, ordered, received, unit_cost_kes, line_total_kes); a cost-gated cell exports EMPTY (absence stays absence in the file, never a hidden zero); the BOM rides; CRLF joins; the boring quoting law reused verbatim from the sheet drill's pure `csvCell`. The file name carries the document: `po-{number}-{status}.csv`.
- **The cost law rides the pipeline**: a PO's shillings (`totalAmount`, per-line `unitCost`/`totalCost`) speak only past `inventory.view.cost` — null renders the honest absence ("the shillings stay behind the cost gate"), never a hidden zero; units and line counts are units — every org role hears them.
- **The provenance rollup** (`src/components/org/restock-dialog.tsx`): a supplier group is a DERIVED bucket — its rows may cite different POs (the hand's moves, the ledger's own drift). When a group's rows cite 2+ distinct hint documents, the divider wears the mono chip "N POs spoke here" whose title names them — the honest form of a bucket's provenance, instead of pretending one document owns the cut.

### Styled
- The pipeline's chip ladder reads cold-to-warm to green as the order travels: slate ink (DRAFT) → amber waiting (PENDING_APPROVAL) → sky green-lit (APPROVED) → indigo on the road (SENT) → cyan acknowledged (CONFIRMED) → amber arriving (PARTIALLY_RECEIVED) → emerald home (RECEIVED) → red grave (CANCELLED); house families only, 3:1 on every face, dark halves from birth. PO numbers wear the mono + tabular-nums face (document numbers, not words — the hint chip's voice grown to the ledger); the branch picker and the finder wear the house focus ring in the ledger's sky hue (distinct from the stocktake's amber and the hand's violet); per-line received progress rides a hairline bar (emerald when the line landed, sky while it's on the road); tabular-nums on every figure; the read-only footer names the law.

## [2.224.0] The spoken hint

The hint names its source, and the sheet goes portable. The supplier hint has a law ("the latest purchase order of ANY status, newest orderDate wins") and a known failure shape (the v2.221 live flattening, where one big PO pointed every row at one supplier and the split died with the groups). A hint that hides its source can only be survived; a hint that shows its receipt can be READ — before it shapes a draft. And the drill's lines, already the org's readable truth, become the yard's portable truth: one CSV, the ring binder's loop closed.

### Added
- **The spoken hint** (`src/app/api/org/kpi-summary/route.ts` + `src/components/org/restock-dialog.tsx`): the named wire's supplier-hint reduction now carries the receipt — `lastSupplierPoNumber` + `lastSupplierAt` (the full wire already spoke `lastPoNumber`/`lastPoDate`; the dialog maps both, one story in both scopes). Every restock row wears a mono `hint: PO-…` chip whose title speaks the law ("the ledger hints the latest purchase order of ANY status, newest first — PO-… named this row (ordered …)"). The flattening is no longer a mystery: the row names the document that flattened it.
- **The sheet's copy** (`src/components/org/sheet-drill.tsx`): the drill grows a CSV export over the lines it ALREADY holds — zero new fetch, the wire untouched. The file speaks the WHOLE sheet (the finder narrows the reading, never the export; the aria names the law); columns product, sku, unit, snapshot, counted, variance, variance_cost_kes; a cost-gated cell exports EMPTY (absence stays absence in the file, never a hidden zero); a CANCELLED sheet exports its void truth (snapshot + uncounted); the button hides on a line-less sheet (an absent control never lies); the BOM rides so Excel opens UTF-8 honestly; CSV quoting per the boring law (commas, quotes and newlines ride wrapped and doubled — a pure `csvCell` the pins can read). File name carries the branch code, the sheet's day and the status: `count-sheet-JUJ-2026-10-27-closed.csv`.

### Styled
- The hint chip is mono + tabular-nums in the muted family at 3:1 (a document number, not a word — distinct from the violet hand and the amber split); the CSV button wears the house border family with the amber focus ring and a decorative download glass; dark halves from birth; tabular-nums on every figure.

## [2.223.0] The hand move

The supplier split answers to the hand as well as the ledger. The v2.221 cut read the hints; but the hints have a known failure shape — seen LIVE in the v2.221 E2E, where one big recent purchase flattened every row's hint to ONE supplier, the groups collapsed, and the toggle's availability died with them. A yard that orders per counter had no way back until new POs re-diversified the ledger. This round gives the hand power with receipts, and the void joins the tamper-evident chain.

### Added
- **The hand move** (`src/components/org/restock-dialog.tsx`): in split mode every draft row carries a "ships via" select — moving a row writes a HAND MOVE for THIS draft only (the ledger's hints stay the default and the record; the move never persists). A moved row wears the violet "moved by hand" chip and a "back on its hint" reset; a group holding moved rows says how many; a global "clear all moves" returns the whole draft to the hints' story; parked moves (split flipped off) are disclosed, never silent. Setting a row back onto its DERIVED place deletes the move — the record holds only differences, the hints' story is restored by re-derivation, never re-written.
- **The grown availability law**: the split toggle now shows whenever the branch's ACTIVE roster names 2+ suppliers, even when the hints flattened to one group — the one-group split speaks its single-group truth ("move lines by hand to cut the draft") instead of hiding. A single-supplier branch still hides the toggle: there is genuinely nowhere to move a line TO (an absent control never lies).
- **The walk's receipts**: a split PO raised from moved rows carries "N lines moved by hand." in its notes — the raised document remembers the hand that shaped it.
