## [2.200.0] The branch row speaks

The org console's KPI row finished its door arc in v2.199.0 with every card speaking — and the assessment that picked this round asked the worklog's own open question: does a BRANCH-level dashboard have a row of quiet tiles with named wires behind them? It does. The branch workspaces' KPI row had three quiet tiles — Today's Revenue, Transactions and Avg Transaction rendered divs with no affordance — even though every story they could tell already rode the dashboard wire: `hourlySalesBreakdown` (the full day, hour by hour), `paymentMethodBreakdown` (a full GROUP BY — the complete tender mix, not a window) and `recentTransactions` (the API's named 10-row window on the day). This release gives the three tiles the door vocabulary and three self-contained focus views — zero new queries, the doors compose the SAME answer the cards show.

### Added
- **The row's three doors** (`dashboard-tab.tsx`): `KpiCard` gains the door vocabulary — an optional `testId` handle, the left **edge bar**, the **live dot** and the **hot ring** (lit only when the day is live; a quiet day stays quiet). Today's Revenue opens **The day's ledger** (`testId="branch-revenue-card"`, emerald edge/dot), Transactions opens **The day's receipts** (`branch-transactions-card`, teal), Avg Transaction opens **The basket ladder** (`branch-atv-card`, amber) — the branch row's own hue story, the family vocabulary's branch face.
- **The day's ledger** (`kpi-doors.tsx`): the revenue headline with its receipts meta, the **16 business-hour bars** (6 AM - 9 PM, the page chart's own window) with a zero hour drawing nothing, the **peak hour spoken twice** (in the bar container's aria-label AND in the `day-ledger-peak` line), the **tender mix** from the full GROUP BY sorted loudest-tender-first with each method's count, total and honest `% of the day` share (a zero-total mix renders NO percentage and says so), and the **latest receipts** mini-roster (newest first, capped at 5) under the honest window note.
- **The day's receipts** (`kpi-doors.tsx`): the count headline with the revenue/ATV composition line, the **receipts-per-hour strip** with the **busiest hour spoken twice** (aria + `day-receipts-busiest`), and the **named roster** newest-first — each row speaks the receipt tail, the stable `HH:MM` clock voice (no locale drift), the method chip (teal), the status chip (`PAID`/`COMPLETED` emerald / every other state calm slate), the customer (or the honest `Walk-in`), the cashier and the amount; the **window note** speaks when the count outruns the wire's 10-receipt window (`the wire's 10-receipt window — N more on the day`) — the count stays the truth.
- **The basket ladder** (`kpi-doors.tsx`): the ATV headline with its composition line, and the day's named receipts sorted into **four size buckets** (under KES 1,000 / 1-5k / 5-20k / over 20k — gaps impossible by construction) each with its count and honest `% of the named` share; a zero bucket bar draws nothing while its cell still speaks its 0; the **window note** names what the ladder reads (`The ladder reads the N receipts named on this answer`). The zero day speaks its line (`basket-ladder-clear`).
- **The house laws hold from birth**: all three panels paint their dark half (`dark:border-slate-700 dark:bg-slate-900` — the branch row's THREE new mounts of the dark-panel law); the doors are self-contained focus views with **no anchors** (the honest-door law — the full sections already live on the page below the row; the door is the story, not a shortcut); null wire rows are filtered by type predicate, never a non-null assertion; and the doors render only inside the FULL KPI row (the server already refuses the revenue payload for limitedView roles; the limited row is untouched).

### Fixed
- **THE ROUND'S CONTRAST FIND — caught before ship** (`kpi-doors.tsx`): the doors' 10px window notes and hour-axis labels were drafted as `text-slate-400` in light — **2.63:1** on the white panel, under the 4.5 bar their small text must meet — with `text-slate-500` in dark (**3.74:1** on slate-900, the same miss on the dark half). The shipped voice is **`text-slate-500 dark:text-slate-400` (4.77:1 / 6.79:1)**, and the draft pair is pinned below the bar in reverse in the round's contrast script so the ghost cannot return. The bars' pastel fills are documented as **DECORATION** per the v2.198.0 house rule (the key figures are aria-labeled on the bar containers AND spoken twice beside: the peak line, the busiest line, every bucket's count + share; the dark fills measure 5.08-5.59:1, the light fills 1.86-2.10:1 by design). `scripts/contrast-math-branch-doors-v2200.mjs`: **28/28**.

### Tests
- NEW `src/tests/branch-kpi-doors-v2200.test.ts`: the wire purity (no fetch, no db, the structural-subset type); the hook-order law (door state before the early return); the three buttons' faces and handlers; the hot-ring honesty gates; the non-door tiles keeping their voices; the three modals' mount and testids; every honesty gate (zero-day lines, zero-total rule, zero-bucket rule, window notes, gaps-impossible buckets); the status/method chip voices; the twice-spoken peak/busiest hours; the stable clock; the no-anchors law; the predicate-filter law; the limited row untouched; the contrast script's ghosts and the docs' numbers; the release face.

## [2.199.0] The pending tile opens the approval desk

The KPI command row has had FIVE voices since v2.198.0 — the Revenue card told the money's story since v2.173.0, the count face found its week, the ledger opened its book, the shelf opened its watchlist and the yard opened its gate — while the Pending tile badge-summed three queues in silence, even though the named PO roster was already on the wire (`suppliers.purchaseOrders`, the Suppliers Expert panel's payload since the Expert panel shipped). This release gives the desk the row's sixth voice — and the row's LAST silent card speaks: the tile becomes the sixth button, the three queues draw their composition bar, and the drill-down speaks the whole desk — zero new queries, the drill-down composes what the payload already carried.

### Added
- **The approval-desk drill-down** (`kpi-row.tsx`): the tile becomes the row's sixth button (`data-testid="pending-card"`) and opens **The approval desk** modal in the face's indigo — the family vocabulary's sixth face (orange revenue, blue transactions, red debt, amber stock, rose rentals, indigo pending) with the **queue** qualifier chip (the roster/ledger/watchlist chips' desk sibling). The card gains the queue **mini bar** (the three queues as one stacked strip: indigo-600 purchase orders → indigo-500 open shifts → indigo-400 expenses — one family, three queues; widths are honest shares of the desk, a zero queue draws nothing, a clean desk draws a calm track) under its count, gated on the desk holding work.
- **The modal's anatomy** (`kpi-row.tsx`): the composition bar at reading size (`data-testid="approval-desk-bar"`, `role="img"` with the full aria-label), the three queue cells (`approval-desk-queue-purchaseOrders/openShifts/expenses`) each with its dot, count and **honest share of the desk**, and the meta line naming the DB-derived action queue the figures compose. **The named PO queue**: pending purchase orders — the roster filtered to the SAME two statuses the count reads (`DRAFT | PENDING_APPROVAL`) — loudest order first (`approval-desk-po-row`) with the status chip (draft calm slate / approval indigo), the expected-date voice (`3d late` amber / `due today` indigo / `due in 3d` slate / **honest `no date set`** — never a fabricated "on time"), the branch line (`Unassigned` never dropped) and the order total; the **cap note** (`approval-desk-po-capped`) speaks when the roster's 8-row window shows fewer pending POs than the count reads — the count stays the truth, the roster a window. Honesty rules: a clean desk renders NO percentage and no strip (`approval-desk-clear`); the two count-only queues speak where their rosters live **in words** (the Shift Scheduling tab / the Financial tab — `approval-desk-shifts-note` / `approval-desk-expenses-note`); an older cached answer without the roster speaks its step-aside (`approval-desk-po-stale`) while the count story above still carries.
- **THE HONEST-DOOR LAW APPLIED — no deep links**: the desk modal is self-contained like its five siblings. A shell-tab deep link would be a role-gated door for some console viewers (INVENTORY_MANAGER cannot read the shift-scheduling or financial tabs), so the pointer lines speak the destination in words instead — the pointer is not a door.
- **QA handle**: the row's first voice gains its findable name — the Revenue card is now `data-testid="revenue-card"` (the transactions/lowstock/overdue/debt/pending cards' sibling).
- **Zero wire changes**: the type contract (`primitives.tsx`) and the route (`kpi-summary`) already carried `pending` (the DB-derived action queue) and `suppliers.purchaseOrders` (supplier, store, status, total, expectedDate; newest-first, capped at 8). The modal re-derives the pending slice, the loudest-first order and the date math client-side from the SAME rows; the one-writer rule holds (one queue array, one share fn, one status-chip fn, one date-chip fn).

### Fixed
- **THE ROUND'S CONTRAST SWEEP — three re-voices, pinned in reverse** (`kpi-row.tsx`, `chrome.tsx`, `store-matrix.tsx`): (1) the revenue modal's lens toggle **ACTIVE GROSS chip rode white-on-emerald-600 — 3.67:1**, under the 4.5 bar its 10px uppercase text must meet, since the toggle shipped in v2.187.0 (the INACTIVE voice always passed at 4.77:1; the violator was the ACTIVE one nobody re-measured after the palette moved). Re-voiced to **emerald-700 (5.37:1)**. (2) The login error line rode **red-600-on-red-50 (4.36:1)** — the exact pairing v2.198.0 found on the Expert OVERDUE badge. Re-voiced to **red-700 (5.88:1)**. (3) The Store Matrix's hot debt figure rode red-600-on-white — **4.76:1 with the installed palette, ABOVE the bar** (the v2.159 knife-edge number came from the older palette), so this is a **family-alignment re-voice** to red-700 (6.42:1): the house's hot REAL text speaks red-700 since v2.159 and the siblings already do. All three pre-fix states are pinned below their bars in reverse in the round's contrast script so the gaps cannot return silently; the org-console mover family (the backlog's audit half) was measured and **already passes** — recorded, no fix owed.

### Tests
- NEW `src/tests/approval-desk-v2199.test.ts`: the desk math executed on the wire's own sample (the pending slice filters to the count's exact statuses, the loudest-order sort, the honest date voice incl. the no-date case, the queue shares 57.1/14.3/28.6, the zero-total rule, the cap rule); the one-writer rule; the card's sixth button/face/qualifier/mini bar + the revenue-card handle; the drill-down's testids, honesty gates, chips, tolerances; the **no-deep-links** law; the six-panel dark-half law; the three re-voices pinned at the source; the release surfaces.
- `scripts/contrast-math-approval-desk-v2199.mjs`: **42/42 families pass** over the REAL composite chain (the sweep fixes + ghosts pinned in reverse; the desk's light faces 4.55–14.00:1; dark 5.72–16.28:1; the mover family recorded passing). The composition-bar fills follow the v2.198.0 decoration law — aria-labeled and spoken twice beside it, deliberately not asserted.
- `scripts/verify-v2-199-0.mjs` wired — fresh verify script per the house convention.

## [2.195.0] The transactions card finds its week

The KPI command row has had ONE voice since v2.173.0: the Revenue card opened the money's drill-down while the Transactions card sat mute beside it — a static tile even though its week (5 checkouts vs 25 the week before, today's zero, the −80% verdict) was already on the wire. This release gives the count face the same voice, and the round's QA found something better: the v2.194.0 dark-voice fix was true to its math and false to its surface — the modal's body panel kept its light paint while the interior wore dark tokens, so dark mode measured 1.05:1 (invisible). Both are fixed here, and the contrast script now derives over the REAL composite chain.

### Added
- **The transactions drill-down** (`kpi-row.tsx`): the card becomes the row's second button (`data-testid="transactions-card"`) and opens a modal mirroring the revenue anatomy — the count spark (380×80, `#2563eb`), the four-cell stat grid (Today / 7d total / Prior 7d / Avg per day), the blue week-over-week strip (label, week figure, arrowed verdict, prior chip — up emerald, down red, flat calm, and a zero prior renders NO percentage), and "The week by branch" rows joined by storeId, loudest count first, each wearing the shared `WeekDeltaChip`. The card wears the face's blue edge + live dot (the family vocabulary the Revenue card speaks in orange) and the **settled** qualifier chip (the v2.185.0 gross-chip's count-face sibling: every number counts the SAME settled rows the Revenue card sums).
- **The wire, still one read** (`kpi-summary` route + `org/summary` route + `primitives.tsx`): `transactions.spark` — the SAME settled rows counted one each into their own buckets in the SAME pass as the gross/net/tax sparks (four faces of one read, zero new queries; integers by construction). `/api/org/summary`'s `perStoreTrend` entries gain `txWeek`/`txPrior` — mapped from the per-store groupBys' `_count._all` that have ridden along since v2.174.0 (the counts were already in memory, waiting). Both additive under NEW names: an older cached answer keeps the legacy card line, the honest spark-absent note, and the branch block's step-aside — never a NaN, never a fabricated shape.
- **The modal ground fix** (`kpi-row.tsx` + `scripts/contrast-math-transactions-lens-v2195.mjs` + `docs/a11y/contrast-math.md`): the v2.194.0 dark tokens were derived over the DIALOG ground, but the modal's body panel (`bg-slate-50`, no dark half) paints between the dialog and every interior token — and the DOM composites against the NEAREST painted ancestor. Measured live: `dark:text-slate-100` values on the unpainted panel = **1.05:1**, the `-300` strip voices 1.54–1.57:1. Both body panels now own `dark:border-slate-700 dark:bg-slate-900` (the lens toggle's dark face since v2.187.0) and the v2.194.0 tokens become true over it. The script proves the bug in reverse (three discovery rows pinned must-stay-under-2:1) and re-derives every family over the REAL chain — dialog → panel → strip → chip — superseding the v2.194.0 numbers. **46/46 families pass** (light 4.55–14.00:1, dark 5.46–16.28:1, the card's white-by-design voice measured too; strokes under the 3:1 non-text bar). ## [2.194.0] The VAT face gets its spark

The revenue modal has spoken three faces since v2.187.0 — the gross till (the lens chart), the net lens (violet chart + its own week-over-week strip since v2.188.0), and the VAT strip (figures + its arrowed week-over-week chip) — but the VAT face stayed mute about its own SHAPE: the two other faces wear 7-day sparks, and the tax collector's rhythm (flat, spiky, seasonal) was invisible behind a single 7d figure. The wire already carried everything needed: the spark rows have selected `taxAmount` since the VAT lens first shipped, and the net buckets are computed as gross − tax per day in the same loop. This release buckets the SAME rows' tax in the SAME pass — zero additional reads — and the VAT strip gains its spark.

### Added
- **The VAT spark** (`kpi-summary` route + `kpi-row.tsx`): the revenue payload grows `taxSpark` (7 per-day tax buckets, round2'd, additive — an older cached answer renders no spark, never a NaN) bucketed in the SAME loop as the gross/net sparks, so the modal's three faces cost ONE query total. The modal renders it under the VAT strip at 380×36 (the lens charts stay the 80px lead) in the face's own amber (`#d97706`) — a THIRD stroke hue so emerald gross, violet net and amber VAT never merge at a glance. The svg is aria-hidden decoration; the strip's figures already speak the face.
- **The modal's dark-mode voice, fixed** (`kpi-row.tsx` + `scripts/contrast-math-revenue-modal-v2194.mjs` + `docs/a11y/contrast-math.md`): the modal was built light-only while its Dialog surface paints `bg-background` — in dark mode the stat grid's `text-slate-800` VALUES measured **1.35:1** (near-invisible), the labels 4.15:1, and the strips' light alpha paints sank under their own text (violet-800 4.13:1, emerald-800 3.54:1). Both strips now wear the house's proven dark chip pattern (`{c}-500/10` ground under a `-300` voice, the `/30` ring), the verdict chips' three states gain their dark halves, the prior/net chips swap `bg-white/80` for `dark:bg-{c}-500/20`, and the grid wears `dark:text-slate-400` labels under `dark:text-slate-100` values. **26/26 families pass** (dark margins 6.37–18.05:1); the VAT spark's stroke is recorded under the 3:1 non-text bar (3.06 light / 6.20 dark) as aria-hidden decoration. The a11y doc's section closes as FIXED — the release's code change, not a reviewed-passing note.

## [2.193.0] The absence cell opens the report

The People & Payroll surfaces have worn their "Absence" cell as plain text with an amber voice since the panel first spoke it (v2.110.0) and the drill-down inherited it (v2.23.0): a branch whose unscheduled absence crosses 10% turns amber and font-semibold — and then goes nowhere. The destination this release opens has existed all along: the HR reports desk already speaks `?tab=absence` (one of its own REPORT_TAB_KEYS) and already honors the v2.23.0 per-branch `storeId` deep link, and the org summary StatCard above the drill-down has linked the org-wide absence report for releases. This release composes what exists: at the 10% attention threshold the rate becomes a door — on the drill-down AND on the shared panel (table and mobile cards through one shared cell) — and the desk report's chip families carry their contrast-math numbers.

### Added
- **The absence door** (`org-console.tsx` drill-down + `people-payroll-by-branch.tsx` shared panel): at `monthAbsenceRatePct >= 10` the rate renders as the house door — `buildAbsenceReportHref(storeId)` → `/hr/reports?tab=absence&storeId=<id>`, the spoken label naming rate and branch ("14% absence rate at Juja Main this month — open the absence report narrowed to Juja Main"), the shared vocabulary in the title, the external-link glyph, and the door affordance (amber alert voice, orange hover, the orange focus ring). The links lib stays the ONE writer of the deep-link shapes — the builder composes the destination's OWN established contract, no wire change, no destination change.
- **The honest threshold**: below 10% the calm plain text stays — the drill affordance appears exactly where the number demands attention, never on a quiet branch. The branch name's headcount link remains the always-on per-branch entry, and the reports page's own tab switcher carries the storeId across tabs, so a calm branch's absence report is two clicks away — the restraint the option's assessment called for, without an additive `?absence=` wire param the employees list would need.
- **No role gate, because a door that 403s is a lie — and this one cannot**: every viewer of the two People & Payroll surfaces (ORG_SUMMARY_ROLES) passes the reports API's guard — SUPER_ADMIN via the hrGuard bypass, STORE_OWNER and ACCOUNTANT are REPORT_ROLES members. The panel's absence cell extracts into ONE `AbsenceCell` (the ExpiryCell/LatestRunCell sibling) so the table and the mobile card render exactly one absence vocabulary.
- **The report families' numbers** (`scripts/contrast-math-desk-report-chips-v2193.mjs` + `docs/a11y/contrast-math.md`): the desk report page's active scope chips, Print, File-all (the family's only light ALPHA `bg-emerald-50/70`), the ledger's via chip, and the page's own four status chips — first numbers over the app BACKGROUND token (not the digest's literal slate-900; the v2.192.0 numbers do not transfer). **23/23 families pass** the 4.5:1 small-text bar; light CANCELLED the tightest status corner (4.55:1, matching the digest's number — the `-50` chip is stack-independent), light file-all hover the family's one new tight corner (4.73:1). Reviewed-passing — no code change, the numbers are the deliverable.

## [2.190.0] The drill-down ranks its payrolls

The org dashboard's HR drill-down has sorted its "Latest payroll" column by `latestPayroll.periodName` TEXT since v2.183.0 — a fragile proxy for recency that breaks across naming formats ("November 2025" sorts after "March 2026" alphabetically) and ignores the one thing the cell actually WEARS: the status badge. The house rule is that a comparator sorts what the cell shows (the alerts cell sorts its SUM), so this release the payroll column sorts by STATUS RANK — the run's finality — and the desk roster's avatar-hue family carries its contrast-math numbers.

### Added
- **The rank ladder** (`hr-drilldown-sort.ts`): `payrollStatusRank` — LOCKED/POSTED share the top rank (both mean the money is settled; the cell paints them the same emerald), then APPROVED, DRAFT, VOIDED (dead work below even a draft); an unknown status reads 0 and never throws (the wire speaks the Prisma enum; a future value sorts beneath the known ladder, never guessed).
- **The payroll column sorts by finality**: primary axis `mul × (rankA − rankB)`; ties break directionally by `createdAt` (desc = the freshest run first, asc = the oldest — `Date.parse`, an unparseable stamp reads as 0, never NaN), then periodName A→Z, then the stable arrival order. The fresh click is **desc** (locked clusters on top — the "which branches are settled vs still open" scan); the asc click is the honest **mirror** (least final first — the "who needs attention" scan). The v2.183.0 absence rule rides untouched: a branch with NO run sorts LAST in both directions. The branches CSV follows the SAME sorted memo — one sort, one truth, pixels and file can never disagree.
- **The honest hint** (`org-console.tsx`): the payroll sort button carries a `title` speaking the rank language ("locked & posted first, then approved, draft, voided; click again for the mirror; no runs last") — the semantics live where the click happens, not in a changelog.
- **The avatar-hue family's numbers** (`scripts/contrast-math-avatar-hues-v2190.mjs` + `docs/a11y/contrast-math.md`): the desk roster's six avatar hues have worn alpha-composited dark chips (`dark:bg-{c}-500/20` over the row's paint) since v2.65.0 — axe's color-contrast INCOMPLETE class, uncomputed until now. The committed script composites the real stacks (calm slate-900, hover slate-800/50, drilled emerald-500/10) from the INSTALLED Tailwind theme and speaks the verdict: **24/24 families pass** the 4.5:1 small-text bar (10px chips and initials), light amber the tightest corner at 4.54:1. Reviewed-passing — no code change, the numbers are the deliverable.

## [2.189.0] The roster keeps its week

v2.186.0 named yesterday's reviewers; v2.188.0 taught the rows to drill. But the panel's week face stayed anonymous — the tiles counted HOW MANY reviewers were active over the trailing 7 days (`weekReviewers`, v2.64.0) and kept the names to themselves, the branches CSV filed only yesterday's roster, and the printable desk report's reviewer rows carried no share at all. This release the roster keeps its week: the read exposes the week's per-reviewer shape, the panel names the week's who, the offline twin files it, and the printable twin wears the share bar.

### Added
- **The week roster on the read** (`review-activity/route.ts`): `stats.weekReviewerRows` — the per-reviewer visit counts (plus corrections answered) over the SAME trailing-7-day window the `weekReviewers` NUMBER has counted since v2.64.0, loudest-first (ties A→Z — the yesterday roster's exact ordering contract), capped at 24 by the same crowded-desk rule. Additive under a NEW name: `weekReviewers` keeps its meaning, no consumer re-keys; an empty window derives to an honest empty array.
- **The week strip** (`panels.tsx`): under the 7-day tiles, "who pulled the week · share of N visits · click a name to pull their file" — the SAME share-bar affordance family as yesterday's roster (avatar hue pill, emerald bar on a muted track, visits · share figure, the hover-chevron drill, `aria-pressed` toggle semantics, the house focus ring), plus the week face's own mark: a per-reviewer clears chip (the corrections this reviewer answered in the window). The slate divider (not the digest's amber) keeps the two faces distinguishable. Renders when the week had visits AND the payload carried the rows — the week face has no top-reviewer chip, so even a solo reviewer is worth naming (their row is the whole story, a 100% bar). Six rows render; a busier week says "+N more" honestly. The strip reads `stats` — org-wide by contract, never the filtered timeline.
- **The CSV's week shape** (`branches-csv.ts`): the desk block grew `week` (visits + roster), and the file grows the "Reviews by reviewer (7d),Reviewer,Visits,Share (%)" sub-section — shares computed BY THE LIB from the week block's OWN visits (round2 of ×100, the wire never trusted), rendered only when the block, its roster and a positive week all arrived (never a divide-by-zero, never an invented 0% column). The export's tolerant parse (`org-console.tsx`) grows the field with the same provably-sound-rows rule; an absent field exports without the sub-section — the file ends at the v2.188.0 shape, never a hole.
- **The report wears the share** (`desk-report-view.tsx` + `desk-report.ts`): the printable twin's reviewer rows now carry the roster strip's signature share-of-visits bar — width = the reviewer's slice of the WINDOW's totals, computed from the report's own derivation — with the top reviewer's bar in the deep emerald the strip gives its drilled row, the % figure spoken beside it (the filed copy carries the share even where backgrounds don't print), and the section caption invites it. The report's own CSV gains the Share (%) column at the END of the Reviewers rows (prior consumers reading columns 1–5 keep reading them unchanged), lib-computed round2 from the report's totals.

## [2.187.0] The modal learns the lens

v2.185.0 reconciled the two revenues: the headline is GROSS of tax on both org surfaces, and the analyst's net/VAT view moved under an explicitly-labeled ribbon. But the lens stayed a strip — one week tax figure, one net figure — while the modal's chart and stat grid spoke only the gross face, and `taxPriorWeekTotal` had no consumer at all. This release the lens deepens into a full second face of the SAME read.

### Added
- **`netSpark` on /api/org/kpi-summary**: the per-day net-of-VAT buckets, filled alongside the gross buckets in the SAME loop over the SAME rows (`sparkRows` has selected `taxAmount` since v2.185.0 — ZERO extra queries, two faces of one read computed in one pass). `spark` keeps its exact gross shape; the two faces never share a variable.
- **`netTodayTotal` and `netPriorWeekTotal`**: the missing net windows, computed from the aggregates the route already fetched (plain number subtraction — `toDec(null) → 0` guards an aggregate without tax). Every cell of the modal's stat grid can now follow the lens, and the ribbon's new week-over-week chip gives `taxPriorWeekTotal` its first consumer.
- **The Gross/Net lens toggle in the revenue drill-down modal** (`kpi-row.tsx`): an accessible grouped control (`role="group"`, `aria-pressed`) that renders ONLY when the payload carried the per-day net buckets — an older cached answer keeps the calm gross modal exactly as v2.185.0 drew it, never a broken control. Gross is the default and stays the contract; the net line wears violet (`#8b5cf6`) so a glance can never mistake the two faces; the title follows the lens ("Revenue trend (gross)" / "Revenue trend (net)"); the net stat grid mirrors the gross grid cell for cell with `??` guards (never a NaN).
- **The honest branch absence in the net lens**: the per-branch week rides `/api/org/summary`, which carries no per-branch tax — so in the net lens the branch rows step aside for one honest line ("net by branch is not on the wire") instead of fabricating numbers. Flip back to Gross to compare branches.
- **The VAT week-over-week chip in the ribbon**: quiet, arrowed, and honest about a zero prior (the WeekDeltaChip honesty rule — no invented %). Emerald when VAT rose, red when it fell, calm when flat.

### Changed
- **docs/multi-store-workspaces.md** trend row records the deepening: the explicit toggle, the zero-extra-query derivation, and the gross headline staying untouched.

### Tests
- NEW `src/tests/revenue-net-lens-v2187.test.ts` (18 tests): the net buckets pinned INTO the same pass, the gross spark pinned UNTOUCHED, the additive names, the tolerance gates, the violet line, the honest branch absence, the WoW honesty rule, and the version.

## [2.186.0] The reviewer roster rides

The desk's morning note answered "how many" and "who won" — the distinct-reviewer count and the top-reviewer chip — but never shipped the FULL per-reviewer roster: the read has computed the `reviewerDigest` map since v2.67.0 and kept the rows to itself. This release the rows ride end to end: the payload exposes them, the digest strip draws them, and the branches CSV files them.

### Added
- **`yesterday.reviewerRows` on /api/org/review-activity**: the SAME map the topReviewer figure reads, every reviewer loudest-first (ties A→Z, the digestTopReviewer ordering), capped at 24 rows so a crowded desk cannot bloat the payload. Additive under a NEW name — the payload's `reviewers` NUMBER (the distinct count, v2.67.0) predates it and keeps its meaning; no consumer re-keys.
- **The share-bar roster in the digest strip** (`panels.tsx`): "who pulled the desk · share of N visits" — every reviewer in their avatar hue with an emerald share-of-visits bar (width = share, capped 100) and their `visits · share%` figure. Renders only when MORE than one reviewer showed (a solo reviewer is the top-reviewer chip's whole story), six rows render, a longer desk says "+N more reviewers on the roster". Optional-field tolerant: an older cached answer keeps the calm strip, never a crash.
- **The "Reviews by reviewer" CSV sub-section** (`branches-csv.ts`): the desk section grows `Reviews by reviewer,Reviewer,Visits,Share (%)` — the offline twin of the roster (the v2.180.0 file-mirrors-screen rule). The share is computed BY THE LIB from the block's own visits (`round(visits/visits × 10000)/100`) — the wire is never trusted; an absent roster or a zero-visit window ends the section above (never a divide-by-zero, never an invented 0% column); RFC-4180 quoting carries comma-bearing names.
- **The export's tolerant parse** (`org-console.tsx`): `yesterday.reviewerRows` rides into the desk block only when `Array.isArray` and every row is provably sound (string name + number visits); an absent field exports WITHOUT the sub-section — the file ends at the v2.185.0 shape.

### Tests
- NEW `src/tests/reviewer-roster-v2186.test.ts`: the derivation + cap + additive naming pinned on the route, the roster gate + share math + "+N more" pinned on the strip, the EXECUTED CSV round-trip (exact shares 50 / 33.33 / 16.67, the comma-name quoting, the absent-roster and zero-visit honesty), the parse tolerance, and the version.
- The six head-pin suites re-anchored with the new counters (75/76/77/108); prior pkg pins ride to 2.186.0 (the treadmill tax).

## [2.185.0] The two revenues agree

Since v2.173.0 the two org surfaces put DIFFERENT numbers under the same "Revenue" label: the owner dashboard's /api/org/summary spoke GROSS of tax (`_sum.totalAmount`, the till face), while the expert console's /api/org/kpi-summary subtracted `taxAmount` and spoke NET — a divergence recorded in v2.173.0, labeled into both CSV exports (v2.178.0/v2.179.0), and carried twice as the top reconciliation pick. This release the reconciliation lands: the expert console now speaks the same gross till face, and the analyst's net/VAT view moves under its own explicit label instead of living as a quiet second meaning of "Revenue".

### Changed
- **`/api/org/kpi-summary` revenue is GROSS of tax**: `today`, `weekTotal`, `priorWeekTotal` and the 7-day sparkline all read `_sum.totalAmount` as-is through a `grossOf` helper; the `netOf` subtraction helper is deleted (structurally pinned out — a tax subtraction on the revenue path can never silently return). The health model's revenue detail now reads "7d gross KES … vs prior …", and the revenueScore compares like with like.
- **The VAT lens rides ADDITIVELY**: the payload gains `kpis.revenue.taxWeekTotal`, `taxPriorWeekTotal` and `netWeekTotal` (gross − tax) computed from the tax sums the aggregates already fetched — no extra query. `KpiSummaryData` types them OPTIONAL so an older cached answer renders the calm gross story, never a crash.

### Added
- **The emerald VAT ribbon in the revenue drill-down modal** (`kpi-row.tsx`): "incl. VAT (7d)" + the net figure under an explicitly-labeled, emerald-bordered row that renders ONLY when the additive fields arrived (`taxWeekTotal != null`) — tolerant by type, never a NaN. The modal retitles "Revenue trend (gross)", the Revenue chip wears a quiet `gross` qualifier pill, and the week-by-branch note retires its net-vs-gross qualifier ("branch shapes and the org line above speak the same gross till face").
- **The recorded history updated everywhere the divergence lived**: the kpi-summary route header gains the REVENUE SEMANTICS block (what changed, why, where the lens went); the org/summary route comment and the `summary-trend.ts` contract header now record divergence → reconciliation; `docs/multi-store-workspaces.md`'s trend row tells the same story; and both CSV note lines (branches CSV, store-matrix CSV) record the AGREEMENT instead of the gap.

### Tests
- NEW `src/tests/revenue-reconciled-v2185.test.ts`: the subtraction pinned OUT (`netOf` forbidden, `.minus(toDec(sums.taxAmount))` forbidden on the route), the headline variables pinned through `grossOf`, the additive fields pinned IN, the ribbon's tolerance and labeling pinned, the one-revenue-contract pinned across BOTH routes, and the CSV/doc/comment updates pinned.
- The five divergence-era suites evolved deliberately: `first-screen-trend-v2173` (route + doc pins now expect the reconciled wording), `expert-week-v2176` and `branch-variance-v2177` (modal copy pins), `branches-csv-week-v2178` and `store-matrix-week-v2179` (CSV note pins).
- The six head-pin suites re-anchored with the new counters (74/75/76/107).

## [2.184.0] The drill-down heals

A post-release E2E pass on the shipped v2.183.0 build caught the one promise the drill-down's memory had not yet kept: a corrupt payload degraded to the calm stock table on load (nothing threw, nothing lied), but the corrupt BYTES stayed in storage until the next owner choice — while both siblings self-heal to an absent key on reload. The gap was structural: the siblings' restore always hands back a FRESH widths object, so their write-back's stock fork runs on every load; the drill-down has no widths by the documented restraint, so a stock-shaped restore changed nothing by reference and the debounced write-back never fired. This release the restore bumps ONE counter — the write-back's post-restore pulse — and the stock fork clears whatever junk it finds, the siblings' self-heal carried over verbatim.

### Fixed
- **The corrupt-byte self-heal (org-console.tsx)**: the restore macrotask now also calls `setHrRestoreTick((t) => t + 1)`, and the write-back's deps carry the tick — ONE post-restore pulse guarantees the stock fork runs after every restore, so corrupt storage is cleared to absent ~250ms after load, exactly as the matrix (v2.181.0) and the branches table (v2.182.0) heal. A valid non-stock restore is unaffected (its deps change anyway; the pulse adds one idempotent re-save); a stock no-storage restore performs one no-op clear.

### Tests
- `hr-drilldown-prefs-v2183`'s write-back pin evolved deliberately (the deps line carries `hrRestoreTick`; the tick's declaration and bump are pinned) with the reasoning written where the pin lives.
- The TEN prior-round suites' package pins evolved to 2.184.0 (the treadmill tax, paid); the six head-pin suites re-anchored with the new counters (73/74/75/106).

## [2.183.0] The drill-down remembers

The org dashboard's HR drill-down — "People & payroll by branch", the console's THIRD dense table since v2.23.0 — has watched its two siblings learn the owner vocabulary (the matrix: sortable, hideable, resizable, remembering in v2.180.0–v2.181.0; the branches table: the full set in v2.182.0) while it stayed stock: arrival order, comfortable rows, and a door that forgot it was ever opened. This release the drill-down joins the vocabulary — a sortable driven header through its OWN pure comparator, a Compact-rows density, and a versioned, corrupt-tolerant localStorage spine under its OWN key that remembers the open door, the sort and the density across reloads.

### Added
- **`src/lib/org/hr-drilldown-sort.ts` (NEW)**: the drill-down's ONE comparator — store-matrix-sort's SIBLING rather than a reuse (the hrPerStore row shares no fields with MatrixSortRow, and a guessed comparator is exactly what the sort libs refuse to be), with the matrix's semantics carried over verbatim: numeric columns (headcount, cost, absence, alerts) loudest-first on a fresh click, text (branch, payroll) A→Z, the payroll column sorting through the SAME latestPayroll join its cells speak with a branch that has NO run sorting LAST in both directions, ties keeping arrival order, null state → arrival order copied, unknown keys refusing to sort.
- **`src/lib/org/hr-drilldown-prefs.ts` (NEW)**: the drill-down's spine — the branches spine's twin under the single key `mbumah.hr-drilldown.prefs` carrying `v: 1` plus `open`, `sort` and `dense`. FOUR fields, not six — THE HONEST RESTRAINT is written into the lib header: the drill-down is a collapsed-by-design section of six ESSENTIAL columns inside a max-h-96 scroll region; hideable columns and drag-resizable widths would be feature bloat on a table nobody has asked to reshape. What an owner actually loses on reload here is the open door, the sort and the density — so those are exactly what the spine carries. Pure (storage INJECTED), CORRUPT-TOLERANT by construction (garbage JSON, unknown version, hostile storage, quota refusal → the calm empty prefs; field-by-field validation keeps whatever is provably sound), BOUNDED (sort key length cap 48).
- **A legal tree for the controls**: the collapsible header row is now a DIV hosting three SIBLINGS — the open/close button (its `aria-expanded` door unchanged), the polite layout note, a conditional **Reset** and the **Compact** toggle. A button can never nest inside a button (invalid HTML, a screen-reader trap), so the v2.182.0 controls join as siblings instead of children.
- **Sortable headers**: every column's header is a real `<button>` driven by the ONE `HR_DRILLDOWN_COLS` spec (six columns, `pad` keeping the stock cell rhythm), `aria-sort` carrying the accessible state and the branches table's OWN `BranchSortGlyph` component reused verbatim — one sort language across ALL THREE dense tables now.
- **The Compact-rows toggle + the conditional Reset**: `aria-pressed` and the orange pressed family marking density active (`[&_td]:px-2 [&_td]:py-1 [&_th]:py-1`); Reset renders ONLY when a non-stock sort or density exists (an exit, not an invite — a stock drill-down shows no Reset at all), resets the in-table tunables (the door's escape is the door's own toggle) and confirms through the polite `org-console-hr-note` line.
- **The export follows the sort**: the branches CSV's People & payroll section now maps the SAME `hrRows` memo the table body reads — the file mirrors what the owner sees (the matrix's v2.180.0 rule, now across all three dense tables).
- **Restore after hydration + the debounced write-back**: stock first paint, saved layout second (the `hrRestored` ref gates the write-back; the restore defers to a macrotask per the react-hooks/set-state-in-effect rule); every choice lands 250ms after the last change settles; a stock layout (door closed, no sort, comfortable rows) CLEARS the key outright; a refused save is silent.
- **The header hint**: the muted column summary now ends "· layout saves to this browser" — the memory is written where the eye already is.
- **Docs**: `multi-store-workspaces.md` gains the drill-down-remembers bullet and the `| HR drill-down · layout memory |` KPI row.

### Tests
- `src/tests/hr-drilldown-prefs-v2183.test.ts` (NEW): both libs EXECUTED (round-trip, exact payload key set, junk dropping, over-long sort key fence, unknown version/garbage/hostile storage → calm defaults, removeItem-less clear, save re-sanitizes; the comparator's numeric defaults, click semantics, the alerts SUM, branch A→Z, the payroll absence-LAST rule in both directions, ties, the unknown-key refusal, the copied-arrival contract), the component contract (both lib imports, the driven spec, the shared glyph, the restore effect source, SSR guards, the debounce + stock-clear fork, the legal-tree header, the Compact + conditional Reset + note, the CSV-sorted-rows wiring, the three-keys isolation), the docs rows, and the release surfaces.
- `console-org-v2152`'s CSV-wiring pin evolved deliberately: the drill-down's section now reads the sorted `hrRows` memo (same intent — the summary payload still feeds it).
- The NINE prior-round suites' package pins evolved to 2.183.0 (the treadmill tax, paid); the six head-pin suites re-anchored by `scripts/reanchor-head-pins.mjs` with the new counters (72/73/74/105).

## [2.182.0] The branches table remembers

The org dashboard's branches table has been the org console's OTHER dense table since v2.149.0, and the Store Intelligence Matrix — its twin across the page — learned to sort (v2.180.0) and to remember (v2.181.0) while it stayed stock: arrival order, fixed columns, comfortable rows, every choice forgotten. This release the branches table learns the matrix's full owner vocabulary — sortable headers, hideable columns, drag-resizable widths and a compact-rows density — and remembers all of it through the SAME kind of versioned, corrupt-tolerant localStorage spine, under the table's OWN key so the two tables never share bytes.

### Added
- **`src/lib/org/branches-table-prefs.ts` (NEW)**: the branches table's spine — the matrix spine's twin (same honesty contract carried over verbatim) under the single key `mbumah.branches-table.prefs` carrying `v: 1` plus `widths`, `visible`, `sort` and `dense`. Pure (storage INJECTED), CORRUPT-TOLERANT by construction (garbage JSON, unknown version, non-object payload, hostile storage, quota refusal → the calm empty prefs; field-by-field validation keeps whatever is provably sound), BOUNDED (widths clamp 40–2000px, entry caps 32, key length 48).
- **`BRANCH_TABLE_COLS` — the ONE column spec**: the driven header, the visibility menu and the resize handler all read the same array (key/label/width/min/align/sortable for the nine columns); `workspace` stays unsortable (an action cell is not data); `week` sorts through the SAME by-storeId join its cells speak.
- **Sortable headers**: every data column's header is a real `<button>` driven by the matrix's OWN comparator (`sortMatrixRows` / `nextSortState` reused verbatim — one sort vocabulary across BOTH dense tables; numbers loudest-first, text A→Z, a branch with no week entry sorts LAST in both directions). The visible state rides the `BranchSortGlyph` (orange arrows when active, a muted chevron pair on hover/focus); the accessible state rides real `aria-sort` values on each th. Null sort → the API's arrival order, copied.
- **The export follows the sort**: `exportBranchesCsv` now maps the SAME `branchRows` memo the table body reads — the file mirrors what the owner sees (the matrix's v2.180.0 rule carried across the page). The other CSV sections keep their own joins.
- **Hideable + resizable columns**: every cell wraps in its column's visibility so a hidden column's td simply does not render; the header carries the matrix's drag-resize separator recipe (per-column min floor, saved width wins over the spec default, the drag lands as ONE debounced save).
- **The Compact-rows toggle**: the fourth tunable dimension — `[&_td]:px-2 [&_td]:py-1 [&_th]:py-1` pulls the padding in without touching a render body; `aria-pressed` carries the state and the orange pressed family marks it active.
- **The Columns menu speaks the memory**: a hint line ("Sort, hidden columns, widths and density save to this browser automatically.") plus a muted **Reset layout** item (RotateCcw) that restores stock and clears the key; the visible, polite `org-console-layout-note` line confirms the actions that have no other surface.
- **Restore after hydration + the debounced write-back**: stock first paint, saved layout second (`branchRestored` ref gates the write-back); every choice lands 250ms after the last change settles; a stock layout CLEARS the key outright; a refused save is silent.

- `trend-doors-v2175`'s header pins evolved deliberately: the branches table's headers are now DRIVEN by `BRANCH_TABLE_COLS`, so the column-order pins moved from literal `<th>` markup to the spec's array order (same intent, one place).

## [2.181.0] The matrix remembers

The Store Intelligence Matrix has been owner-tunable since v2.18.3 — drag-resizable columns, hideable columns — and sortable since v2.180.0, but every one of those choices lived in component state and died with the panel: a reload, a tab switch, or a navigation reset the owner's matrix back to stock. This release the matrix remembers: ONE versioned, corrupt-tolerant localStorage spine carries every owner-tunable choice across reloads, plus a fourth tunable dimension (compact-rows density) and a visible escape hatch (Reset layout).

### Added
- **`src/lib/org/store-matrix-prefs.ts` (NEW)**: the ONE spine the matrix's memory speaks — `loadMatrixPrefs` / `saveMatrixPrefs` / `clearMatrixPrefs` / `parseMatrixPrefs` / `serializeMatrixPrefs` around the single key `mbumah.store-matrix.prefs` carrying `v: 1` plus `widths`, `visible`, `sort` and `dense`. Pure (storage INJECTED — the component hands `window.localStorage` guarded by its own `typeof window` check), so the spine runs under node test runners and stays honest under SSR. CORRUPT-TOLERANT by construction: garbage JSON, an unknown version, a non-object payload, a hostile storage or a quota refusal all degrade to the calm empty prefs — field-by-field validation keeps whatever is provably sound and drops the rest; widths clamp into a 40–2000px band with capped entries so a poisoned payload can never balloon storage or feed the layout engine nonsense.
- **Restore after hydration**: the server renders the stock matrix; the client's first paint matches it; THEN the saved layout takes over — a hydration mismatch is a bug, default-then-restore is a feature. The restore runs once on mount (`restored` ref gates the write-back so the restore's own setState calls are never mistaken for owner choices).
- **The debounced write-back**: every owner choice lands 250ms after the last change settles (a column drag repaints dozens of widths; storage takes ONE). A stock layout CLEARS the key outright — "nothing saved" is the honest shape of "no customizations" — anything else saves the whole payload through the lib, the ONE writer of the bytes. A refused save is silent: the matrix forgets on the next reload exactly as it always did, and never nags.
- **The Compact-rows toggle**: the header gains a fourth tunable dimension — compact rows pull cell padding in (`py-1`) and lower the header band (`h-8`), roughly 30% more rows on screen without touching a render body; the active state speaks in the console's orange accent with `aria-pressed` carrying it to screen readers, and the tooltip says the choice is saved with the layout.
- **The Columns menu speaks the memory**: a hint line ("Widths, hidden columns, sort and density save to this browser automatically.") tells the owner the persistence exists, and a muted **Reset layout** item (RotateCcw icon, `text-slate-500`) restores stock and clears the stored key — an exit, not an invite.
- **Docs**: `multi-store-workspaces.md` gains the matrix-remembers bullet and the `| Matrix · layout memory |` KPI row.

### Tests
- `src/tests/store-matrix-prefs-v2181.test.ts` (NEW): the lib EXECUTED (round-trip serialize→parse, exact payload key set, clamping, entry caps, junk-width/visible/sort dropping, unknown version and garbage JSON → empty prefs, hostile storage throws → false/empty, removeItem-less clear → empty payload written, stock-layout clear wiring, the 250ms debounce shape), the component contract (restore-on-mount source, the `restored` gate, stock-clear vs save fork, Compact button with `aria-pressed` + orange pressed state, the dense Table override, the hint line, the Reset item, lib-is-the-one-writer), the docs rows, and the release surfaces.
- The SEVEN prior-round suites' package pins evolved to 2.181.0 (the treadmill tax, paid); the six head-pin suites re-anchored by `scripts/reanchor-head-pins.mjs` with the new counters (70/71/72/103).

## [2.180.0] The matrix learns to sort

The Store Intelligence Matrix has been the expert console's densest table since v2.18.3 — resizable, hideable, exportable (v2.179.0's offline twin) — but its rows only ever appeared in the API's arrival order: an analyst scanning for the loudest debt or the busiest week had to do the scanning by eye. This release every data column's header becomes a real sort button, driven by ONE pure comparator shared by the table body AND the CSV export — the pixels and the file can never disagree about what "sorted" means.

### Added
- **`src/lib/org/store-matrix-sort.ts` (NEW)**: the ONE comparator the matrix speaks — `sortMatrixRows(rows, state, weekOf?)` plus `nextSortState` (same column flips, new column starts at its default), `defaultDirFor` (numeric columns loudest-first, text A→Z) and `toggledDir`. Pure: no React, no DOM, no clock; null state → arrival order, copied. SORT SEMANTICS: the week column sorts by `weekTotal` through the caller's join, and a store with NO trend entry sorts LAST in BOTH directions — honest absence never wins a loudest-first sort and never hides as a zero at the bottom of a smallest-first one (the v2.175.0 dash rule carried into ordering). Ties keep arrival order (`Array.sort` is stable); an unknown key returns arrival order (the lib never guesses a comparator).
- **Sortable headers**: every data column's header is a real `<button>` (the Actions label stays a plain span — nothing to sort there). Click sorts, click again flips; the resize separator stays OUTSIDE the button so drag and click can never fight. The visible state rides `SortGlyph` — lucide `ArrowUp`/`ArrowDown` in the console's orange accent when active, a muted `ChevronsUpDown` that only appears on hover or keyboard focus (`opacity-0 group-hover:opacity-100 group-focus-visible:opacity-100` — a whisper, not a shout); the active column's label gains `font-semibold text-slate-900`. The accessible state rides `aria-sort` on each th (`ascending`/`descending`/`none` — real ARIA values, never the shorthand).
- **The export follows the sort**: `exportCsv` reads the SAME `sortedStores` memo the table body reads — the file mirrors what the owner sees, arrival order when nothing is sorted (the v2.178.0 "offline twin" philosophy completed).
- **TOLERANT by construction**: the sort state is component-local (resets with the panel, like widths and visibility); a store row satisfies the lib's structural `MatrixSortRow` shape as-is; a nullish trend block just means every week reads absent → the week column's honest-absence rule covers the whole table.
- **Docs**: `multi-store-workspaces.md` gains the matrix-sort bullet and the `| Matrix · sort |` KPI row.

### Tests
- `src/tests/store-matrix-sort-v2180.test.ts` (NEW): the comparator EXECUTED (numeric desc/asc defaults, text asc/desc, the flip via `nextSortState`, the week join through `weekOf`, absence LAST in both directions, stable ties, null state → copied arrival order, unknown key → untouched order, single-row and empty tables), the component contract (sort state + memo + the one-comparator wiring, sortable buttons excluding Actions, glyph vocabulary + aria-hidden, aria-sort real values, the separator outside the button, export-reads-the-sorted-rows), the docs rows, and the release surfaces.
- The SIX prior-round suites' package pins evolved to 2.180.0 (the treadmill tax, paid by hand — now including v2.179.0's own suite); the six head-pin suites re-anchored by `scripts/reanchor-head-pins.mjs` with the new counters (69/70/71/102).

## [2.179.0] The matrix reads the week

Three surfaces speak every branch's week — the dashboard's branches table (v2.175.0/2.177.0), the expert console's store strip (v2.176.0/2.177.0), and the branches CSV (v2.178.0) — but the Store Intelligence Matrix, the expert console's densest table, stayed week-blind: ten columns of today with no shape, no verdict, no prior. This release the matrix reads the week: a "Week 7d" column joins the table, its CSV export becomes the table's offline twin, and the workspace dialog speaks the week at the top. Still ZERO new queries — the `perStoreTrend` block has ridden every summary response since v2.174.0, and the page already polls it.

### Added
- **`src/lib/org/store-matrix-csv.ts` (NEW)**: the matrix's CSV bytes move out of the component into a PURE lib (no db, no network, no DOM — the generated stamp is passed in), and grow the week inline: `Week 7d (KES)`, `Prior week (KES)` and `Change (%)` ride after `Revenue`, the matrix's own reading order. `buildStoreMatrixCsv(stores, generatedAt, trend)` joins the trend block by `storeId` (never row order); numbers export raw (no thousands separators) under RFC-4180 quoting; the header is stable — a store with no trend entry, or a nullish/empty block, leaves its week cells EMPTY (the honesty lives in the cells, the columns never flicker).
- **The Change % is the SAME `weekDelta` verdict the chips speak** (imported from `summary-trend`, never re-derived): signed, round2'd, EMPTY when there is no comparison (missing/undefined/zero/negative prior), `0` for an exactly flat week — chip and file can never disagree. A closing note carries the v2.173.0 gross-vs-net divergence label into the file (`Note,Store week shapes are gross revenue — the expert console's revenue KPI is net of VAT`) and rides only when the trend block is present and non-empty.
- **The matrix's `Week 7d` column**: each cell stacks the store's gross week total (the strip's own "7-day revenue KES X" number, tabular), the muted 64×18 spark, and the `WeekDeltaChip` — the branches table's exact vocabulary sized to the cell; the tooltip composes `7-day revenue KES X (gross) · prior KES Y` when a prior exists; the accessible words ride `weekDeltaWords` as sr-only sentences; a store with no entry renders the calm aria-hidden dash. The column participates in visibility, drag-resize, and the header's new hover hint (`7-day gross revenue, week ending today`).
- **The workspace dialog speaks the week**: opening a store from the matrix renders the week at the top of the POS tab — shape, total, verdict in one orange-accent row (dark pairs kept), same tooltip, same sr-only clauses; no week in the block → the row never renders.
- **The export toast counts the week**: `Exported store_intelligence.csv · week by store` when the block rode, plain otherwise — the owner can tell at a glance whether the week made the file.
- **`StoreMatrix` gains the tolerant `trend` prop** (optional + nullable, `StoreMatrixTrendEntry` exported): the console page passes `summary.perStoreTrend` straight through — no second fetch, an older cached answer degrades to dashes, never a crash.
- **Docs**: `multi-store-workspaces.md` gains the matrix-week bullet and the `| Matrix · week by store |` KPI row.

### Tests
